Plan a major expansion of the DataClaw project with five deliverables:

1. Periodic automatic runs — a `dataclaw auto` non-interactive command + one-time `dataclaw enable-auto` setup + `dataclaw install-schedule --time "HH:MM"` that writes a launchd plist on macOS (systemd timer on Linux). Replaces the human `confirm` gate with a policy check.

2. PII detection via the openai/privacy-filter model (1.5B total, 50M active MoE token classifier, ~750MB–3GB, runs on MPS). Augments — does not replace — the existing regex/entropy redaction in dataclaw/secrets.py and dataclaw/anonymizer.py. The new/known finding distinction drives the auto-mode policy.

3. Switch HF upload from overwriting a single `conversations.jsonl` to source-partitioned dated shards in a unified per-user repo: `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`. Track `last_export_cutoff` in config. Only export "cooled" sessions (source file mtime > 24h old). Declare HF `configs:` block in dataset README so `load_dataset(..., "claude")` etc. works. Leave existing root-level `conversations.jsonl` in place for back-compat and glob both.

4. User-defined folder organization: config entry `folder_rules: {buckets: {work: [...], oss: [...]}, default_bucket: "personal"}`. Projects map to buckets; shards land at `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`. Default bucket when unset: files stay at `{source}/...`.

5. Package as a MacBook app using Tauri — a thin GUI over the CLI that edits config, shows last run + findings, has a "run now" button, lives in the menu bar, and manages the launchd plist. Privacy-filter model downloads on first auto-run (not bundled in the .dmg).

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: DataClaw — Auto runs, privacy-filter PII, partitioned HF uploads, folder rules, Tauri menubar app

## Overview

DataClaw today is a Python CLI that discovers coding-agent sessions (claude/codex/gemini/opencode/openclaw/kimi/custom), anonymizes paths & usernames (`dataclaw/anonymizer.py`), runs regex + entropy secret redaction (`dataclaw/secrets.py`), writes a single flat `dataclaw_conversations.jsonl`, and pushes three files to an HF dataset repo: `conversations.jsonl`, `metadata.json`, `README.md` (`dataclaw/cli.py:409–456`). Config is a TypedDict persisted at `~/.dataclaw/config.json` (`dataclaw/config.py:8–54`). The interactive publish gate is `confirm` (`dataclaw/cli.py:941–1108`) — three text attestations + full-name scan + regex PII scan — which advances `stage="confirmed"`, after which `export` (`dataclaw/cli.py:1329–1595`) pushes.

This plan replaces the human `confirm` gate with a policy check so runs can happen automatically on a schedule; augments regex redaction with the `openai/privacy-filter` MoE token classifier (new findings drive the policy); splits one flat JSONL into `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` dated shards with incremental cutoff; lets the user group projects into buckets; and wraps all of it in a Tauri menubar app that edits config, shows last run, and manages a launchd plist.

**Hard constraints**
- Never regress the existing manual `confirm` → `export` path; auto-mode is additive.
- Privacy-filter is opt-in via an optional extra (`dataclaw[pii]`); the CLI must still function without `torch`/`transformers`.
- Backward compat: existing HF repos with only a root `conversations.jsonl` must keep loading (`load_dataset(repo)` no-config form) after the switch to sharded layout.
- Tauri app is a thin GUI over the CLI — no duplicated business logic; all operations shell out to `dataclaw <cmd>` and parse the trailing `---DATACLAW_JSON---` block.

**Two open decisions — recommendations baked in, questions raised separately so they can be overridden before merge:**
1. Auto-mode default policy: **strict** (any new privacy-filter finding not in the known set blocks push; CLI exits non-zero; notification fires). Rationale: publishing is irreversible and the whole point of the filter is to catch what the regex pipeline missed — permissive defeats that by auto-redacting findings the user never saw. Permissive is available via `--policy permissive` and `dataclaw enable-auto --policy permissive`.
2. Folder rules spec style: **explicit per-project list** as the primary form (`folder_rules.buckets.{bucket: [project_dir_name, ...]}`), plus an optional tag layer for power users (`project_tags.{project: [tag, ...]}` + `folder_rules.buckets_by_tag.{bucket: [tag, ...]}`) resolved as a fallback. Rationale: the repo has no existing project-tag concept, so tag-only would be a from-scratch taxonomy the user has to build before anything works; explicit list lets someone bucket three projects in 30 seconds.

### Step 3: Add cooled-session filter (`dataclaw/parser.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** a new helper near the top of `dataclaw/parser.py` (after the source constants at line 23):
   ```python
   COOL_DOWN_SECONDS = 24 * 60 * 60

   def _is_cooled(session_file: Path, now: float | None = None) -> bool:
       """True if the source file hasn't been touched in the last 24h."""
       try:
           mtime = session_file.stat().st_mtime
       except OSError:
           return False
       return (now or time.time()) - mtime >= COOL_DOWN_SECONDS
   ```
2. **Wire** `_is_cooled` into each file-based parser's session emission loop. Call sites (approximate): `_parse_claude_session_file`:682–706; `_parse_gemini_session_file`:898+; `_parse_openclaw_session_file`:984+; `_parse_kimi_session_file`:1671+; `_parse_custom_sessions`:370–417; `_parse_codex_session_file`:1259+. For each, skip the session if `cooled_only and not _is_cooled(path)`.
3. **For SQLite-backed `opencode`** (`_discover_opencode_projects`:220–239 and `_parse_opencode_session`:536–625), derive "last activity" from the session's newest message timestamp in the DB row and gate on `(now - last_msg_ts) >= COOL_DOWN_SECONDS`.
4. **Thread** a new kwarg `cooled_only: bool = False` through `parse_project_sessions` (`dataclaw/parser.py:420–533`) so callers opt in. The manual `export` path leaves it `False` (preserves current behavior); auto-mode sets it `True`.
5. **Also thread** an optional `since: dict[str, str] | None = None` where the dict is the `last_export_cutoff` map. Inside each parser, compare the parsed session's `end_time` to `since.get(source)`; drop anything older-or-equal.

2. **Return** a `max_end_time_by_source: dict[str, str]` in the metadata dict returned at line 397, computed by tracking the max `session["end_time"]` per source as rows are written. Callers use this to update `last_export_cutoff` after a successful push (not before — cutoff only advances on push success).

## Phase 2: Source-partitioned, date-sharded HF uploads

### Step 6: Output path resolver (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** a new helper near the top of `_run_export` (before `dataclaw/cli.py:1512`):
   ```python
   def _resolve_shard_path(root: Path, session: dict, config: DataClawConfig) -> Path:
       source = session.get("source", "claude")
       project = session.get("project") or "<unknown>"
       bucket = _bucket_for_project(project, config)  # see Phase 3
       day = session["end_time"][:10]  # YYYY-MM-DD UTC from ISO
       parts = ([bucket] if bucket else []) + [source, f"{day}.jsonl"]
       return root.joinpath(*parts)
   ```
2. **Refactor** `export_to_jsonl` (`dataclaw/cli.py:346`) to accept a `root_dir: Path` instead of a single `output_path: Path`, open shard file handles lazily in a `dict[Path, TextIO]`, and close them all in a `finally`. Each `session` is routed via `_resolve_shard_path`. Preserve the `output_path`-single-file mode for CLI `--output file.jsonl` (manual path) via a `legacy_single_file: Path | None` kwarg that, when set, overrides shard resolution and writes one file (back-compat for scripts).
3. **Default** `root_dir` for auto-mode is `~/.dataclaw/export/` (staging dir). Manual `export` keeps default behavior of writing to CWD but now also writes a `dataclaw_export/` subdir alongside the single file when `--sharded` is passed; push path walks it.

## Phase 3: Folder rules / buckets

### Step 10: Bucket resolver (`dataclaw/cli.py` — new helper)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** near the top of cli.py (after the source-choice helpers around line 62):
   ```python
   def _bucket_for_project(project: str, config: DataClawConfig) -> str | None:
       rules = config.get("folder_rules") or {}
       for bucket, projects in (rules.get("buckets") or {}).items():
           if project in projects:
               return bucket
       tags = (config.get("project_tags") or {}).get(project, [])
       if tags:
           for bucket, bucket_tags in (rules.get("buckets_by_tag") or {}).items():
               if any(t in bucket_tags for t in tags):
                   return bucket
       return rules.get("default_bucket")  # may be None → flat layout under source/
   ```
2. **Call** this helper from `_resolve_shard_path` (Step 6) and nowhere else — keep bucket logic single-sourced.

   - `--default-bucket NAME` — sets `folder_rules.default_bucket`.
   - `--tag-project "project=tag1,tag2"` — sets `project_tags[project]`.
   - `--bucket-by-tag "bucket=tag1,tag2"` — sets `folder_rules.buckets_by_tag[bucket]`.
2. **Handle** these in `_handle_config` (`dataclaw/cli.py:1306–1326`) by dispatching to a new `configure_folder_rules(...)` helper that mutates `config["folder_rules"]` / `config["project_tags"]` and calls `save_config`.

## Phase 4: Privacy-filter PII detection

### Step 13: New module `dataclaw/privacy_filter.py`
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `dataclaw/privacy_filter.py` with a lazy, import-guarded surface:
   ```python
   from __future__ import annotations
   import hashlib, json
   from dataclasses import dataclass
   from pathlib import Path

   MODEL_ID = "openai/privacy-filter"
   _MODEL = None  # cached on first call

   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class Finding:
       kind: str       # "PERSON" | "EMAIL" | "PHONE" | "ADDRESS" | ...
       text: str
       start: int
       end: int
       score: float
       session_id: str
       field: str      # "messages[3].content" etc.

       def fingerprint(self) -> str:
           return hashlib.sha256(f"{self.kind}|{self.text}".encode()).hexdigest()[:12]

   def is_available() -> bool:
       try:
           import torch, transformers  # noqa
           return True
       except ImportError:
           return False

   def _load(device: str = "auto"):
       global _MODEL
       if _MODEL is not None:
           return _MODEL
       import torch
       from transformers import AutoModelForTokenClassification, AutoTokenizer
       if device == "auto":
           device = "mps" if torch.backends.mps.is_available() else "cpu"
       tok = AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained(MODEL_ID)
       mdl = AutoModelForTokenClassification.from_pretrained(MODEL_ID).to(device)
       mdl.eval()
       _MODEL = (tok, mdl, device)
       return _MODEL

   def scan_session(session: dict, device: str = "auto", min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]:
       """Run the MoE token classifier over every user/assistant/tool text in a session."""
       ...
   ```
2. **Implement** `scan_session` to walk the same fields as `secrets.redact_session` (`dataclaw/secrets.py:255–273`) — `messages[].content`, `messages[].thinking`, `tool_uses[].input/output` — tokenize, batch through the model (respecting MPS memory: chunk at 512-token windows), decode IOB spans back to char offsets, emit `Finding`s above `min_score`.
3. **Add** `scan_jsonl(path: Path, ...) -> list[Finding]` that streams the export file and aggregates findings across sessions.
4. **Key design rule**: privacy-filter never mutates sessions. It produces findings; the decision of whether to redact is separate (see Step 15).

### Step 14: Known-findings registry (`dataclaw/privacy_filter.py`, `dataclaw/config.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** to `privacy_filter.py`:
   ```python
   def diff_findings(findings: list[Finding], known: dict) -> tuple[list[Finding], list[Finding]]:
       new, seen = [], []
       for f in findings:
           (seen if f.fingerprint() in known else new).append(f)
       return new, seen

   def record_findings(findings: list[Finding], known: dict) -> dict:
       from datetime import datetime, timezone
       now = datetime.now(tz=timezone.utc).isoformat()
       for f in findings:
           fp = f.fingerprint()
           entry = known.get(fp, {"kind": f.kind, "preview": f.text[:40], "first_seen": now, "times_seen": 0})
           entry["times_seen"] += 1
           known[fp] = entry
       return known
   ```
2. **The fingerprint** intentionally omits offsets and field so the same `"Jane Doe"` hit in two sessions collapses to one entry.

### Step 15: Wire privacy-filter into `confirm` (`dataclaw/cli.py:1033–1034`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** to the existing PII scan call at `dataclaw/cli.py:1034`:
   ```python
   pii_findings = _scan_pii(file_path)
   pf_findings, pf_new, pf_seen = [], [], []
   config_pf = config.get("privacy_filter") or {}
   if config_pf.get("enabled"):
       from dataclaw import privacy_filter as pf
       if pf.is_available():
           pf_findings = pf.scan_jsonl(file_path, device=config_pf.get("device", "auto"))
           pf_new, pf_seen = pf.diff_findings(pf_findings, config.get("known_findings", {}))
   ```
2. **Surface** `pf_new` in the JSON output under `"privacy_filter_new_findings"` with a preview (kind, first 40 chars, session ID). Keep `pf_seen` in a second key so the model output is fully visible.
3. **After** the existing `config["review_verification"]` block (`dataclaw/cli.py:1039–1044`), record the new finding hashes: `config["known_findings"] = pf.record_findings(pf_new + pf_seen, config.get("known_findings", {}))`. This is the manual "ack" — the user saw it in `confirm`, it's now known.
4. **Add** a `--policy {strict,permissive}` flag (default strict) on `confirm` for parity with `auto`; strict mode exits non-zero when `pf_new` is non-empty and the user hasn't passed `--ack-privacy-findings` (a new boolean). This keeps manual `confirm` a superset of auto policy semantics.

### Step 16: Optional-dep gating + first-run download
**Scope:** Small
1. **In CLI**, emit a structured error if the user tries to use privacy-filter without `dataclaw[pii]` installed:
   ```python
   if not pf.is_available():
       print(json.dumps({"error": "privacy-filter requires optional deps", "fix": "pip install 'dataclaw[pii]'"}, indent=2)); sys.exit(2)
   ```
2. **Model download** happens on first `pf._load()` via HF Hub's default cache (`~/.cache/huggingface/hub/`); emit a one-time stderr message "Downloading openai/privacy-filter (~1.5GB)…" so users know why the first run is slow. No bundling.

**Scope:** Medium

   - `test_fingerprint_deterministic_same_text`

## Phase 5: Auto mode + OS scheduling

### Step 18: `dataclaw enable-auto` command (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** new subparser in `main()` (`dataclaw/cli.py:1189`):
   ```python
   ea = sub.add_parser("enable-auto", help="One-time setup: turn on non-interactive auto-publish with a blanket attestation.")
   ea.add_argument("--publish-attestation", required=True, help="Text attestation for all future auto-publishes.")
   ea.add_argument("--policy", choices=["strict", "permissive"], default="strict")
   ea.add_argument("--full-name", default=None, help="Used for recurring full-name scans.")
   ea.add_argument("--skip-full-name-scan", action="store_true")
   ea.add_argument("--enable-privacy-filter", action="store_true")
   ```
2. **Handler** `enable_auto(args)`:
   - Re-use `_collect_review_attestations` semantics but accept blanket text via flags.
   - Enforce `stage in {"confirmed", "done"}` as a precondition (user has run through at least once manually) to prevent auto-publishing a brand-new install blind.
   - Save to `config["auto"] = {"enabled": True, "policy": args.policy, "publish_attestation": args.publish_attestation, "full_name": ..., "skip_full_name_scan": ..., "enabled_at": now}`.
   - If `--enable-privacy-filter`: set `config["privacy_filter"]["enabled"] = True` and warmup-import to validate the optional dep is present; if not, fail with the install fix-it hint from Step 16.
   - Emit JSON with `next_command: "dataclaw install-schedule --time 03:00"`.

### Step 19: `dataclaw auto` command (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** subparser `au = sub.add_parser("auto", help="Non-interactive export + policy-gated push.")` with `--force` (ignore policy block and push anyway — emits a warning), `--dry-run` (export shards but don't push), `--policy-override` (runtime-only override of config policy).
2. **Handler** `auto(args)`:
   ```python
   config = load_config()
   if not (config.get("auto") or {}).get("enabled"):
       fail_json({"error": "Auto mode not enabled", "fix": "dataclaw enable-auto --publish-attestation '…'"})
   # 1. Export with cooled_only=True, since=config.get("last_export_cutoff", {})
   # 2. If zero sessions exported: record last_auto_run={"result":"noop"}, exit 0.
   # 3. Run regex/entropy scan as today (_scan_pii) — these are auto-redacted inline by export_to_jsonl.
   # 4. If privacy_filter.enabled: scan, diff vs known, route by policy:
   #      strict + pf_new non-empty + not args.force → write last_auto_run={"result":"blocked", "new_findings": len(pf_new), "preview":[...]}, notify (Step 20.5), exit 3.
   #      permissive → append all pf_findings to config["redact_strings"], re-run redaction on staging shards, record fingerprints as known, continue.
   # 5. Push via push_to_huggingface(root_dir, repo_id, meta).
   # 6. On success: update config["last_export_cutoff"] from meta["max_end_time_by_source"], config["last_auto_run"]={"result":"pushed", ...}, save_config.
   ```
3. **Policy enforcement** lives in a single helper `_evaluate_auto_policy(pf_new, config, args) -> Literal["proceed","block"]` so it's testable in isolation.
4. **Exit codes**: 0 success/noop, 2 missing deps, 3 policy block, 4 HF push failure (distinct so the scheduler / Tauri app can tell them apart).

### Step 20: `dataclaw install-schedule` command (`dataclaw/cli.py`, `dataclaw/scheduler.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `dataclaw/scheduler.py` with platform dispatch:
   ```python
   PLIST_LABEL = "io.dataclaw.auto"
   SYSTEMD_UNIT = "dataclaw-auto"

   def install(time_hhmm: str, platform: str = sys.platform) -> Path: ...
   def uninstall(platform: str = sys.platform) -> None: ...
   def status(platform: str = sys.platform) -> dict: ...
   ```
2. **macOS** (`darwin`): write `~/Library/LaunchAgents/io.dataclaw.auto.plist` with `StartCalendarInterval` at `{Hour, Minute}` parsed from `--time HH:MM`, `ProgramArguments=[str(Path(sys.executable)), "-m", "dataclaw", "auto"]`, `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` pointing at `~/.dataclaw/logs/auto.{out,err}.log`, `RunAtLoad=false`. After writing, call `launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) ~/Library/LaunchAgents/io.dataclaw.auto.plist` (fallback to `launchctl load` on older macOS). Handle "already loaded" by `launchctl bootout` first.
3. **Linux** (`linux`): write `~/.config/systemd/user/dataclaw-auto.service` with `ExecStart=%h/.local/bin/dataclaw auto` and `~/.config/systemd/user/dataclaw-auto.timer` with `OnCalendar=*-*-* HH:MM:00`. Call `systemctl --user daemon-reload && systemctl --user enable --now dataclaw-auto.timer`.
4. **Other platforms**: emit JSON error "scheduling not supported on {platform}; run `dataclaw auto` from your own scheduler."
5. **Add** subparsers:
   - `is = sub.add_parser("install-schedule"); is.add_argument("--time", required=True)`.
   - `sub.add_parser("uninstall-schedule")`.
   - `sub.add_parser("schedule-status")` — reports whether the unit is loaded and next-fire time (`launchctl list | grep io.dataclaw.auto`; `systemctl --user list-timers`).
6. **Never** edit the plist/timer in place — always rewrite; makes re-run safe.

### Step 21: Notifications on blocked auto-run (`dataclaw/scheduler.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `notify(title: str, body: str)` using `osascript -e 'display notification …'` on macOS, `notify-send` on Linux. Swallow failures — a failed notification must not fail the auto-run.
2. **Called** from `auto()` on policy block (Step 19.4.a) with `title="DataClaw blocked"` and body listing new-finding count + "run `dataclaw confirm`".

### Step 22: Tests — auto mode + scheduling (`tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_scheduler.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`test_auto_requires_enable_auto`** — fresh config without `auto.enabled` → exit 2.
2. **`test_auto_noop_when_no_new_sessions`** — empty staging dir → exit 0, `last_auto_run.result == "noop"`.
3. **`test_auto_blocks_in_strict_mode`** — monkey-patch privacy-filter to return one new finding, strict policy → exit 3, `last_auto_run.result == "blocked"`, no push call.
4. **`test_auto_permissive_redacts_and_pushes`** — same setup, permissive → push called, `redact_strings` contains the offending text, `last_export_cutoff` advances.
5. **`test_auto_policy_override_flag`** — runtime `--policy-override permissive` with strict in config → push.
6. **`test_schedule_install_writes_plist`** (macOS-only via `sys.platform` monkey-patch) — asserts plist file contents, especially `StartCalendarInterval` keys.
7. **`test_schedule_install_writes_systemd_unit`** (Linux-only) — asserts `.timer` file contains the parsed `OnCalendar` stanza.
8. **Skip** the actual `launchctl`/`systemctl` invocation in tests — monkey-patch `subprocess.run`.

## Phase 6: Tauri menubar app

### Step 23: Scaffold Tauri 2 project (`app/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** `app/` at repo root with `pnpm create tauri-app@latest` — template: React + TypeScript + Vite. Result:
   ```
   app/
     src-tauri/
       tauri.conf.json
       Cargo.toml
       src/main.rs
       src/dataclaw.rs   # our thin CLI wrapper
       src/schedule.rs   # plist read/write
     src/
       App.tsx
       routes/{Dashboard.tsx, Config.tsx, Findings.tsx}
       lib/api.ts        # invoke() calls into Rust
     package.json
     vite.config.ts
   ```
2. **Add** `tauri.conf.json` tray config:
   ```json
   {"app": {"trayIcon": {"iconPath": "icons/tray.png", "iconAsTemplate": true, "menuOnLeftClick": false}}}
   ```
3. **Add** `.gitignore` entries for `app/node_modules`, `app/src-tauri/target`.

### Step 24: Rust backend — CLI bridge (`app/src-tauri/src/dataclaw.rs`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** Rust commands that shell out to the `dataclaw` CLI and parse `---DATACLAW_JSON---` blocks:
   ```rust
   #[tauri::command]
   async fn dataclaw_status() -> Result<serde_json::Value, String> {
       run_cli(&["status"]).await
   }

   #[tauri::command]
   async fn dataclaw_config_get() -> Result<serde_json::Value, String> {
       run_cli(&["config"]).await
   }

   #[tauri::command]
   async fn dataclaw_auto_now(force: bool) -> Result<serde_json::Value, String> {
       let mut args = vec!["auto"];
       if force { args.push("--force"); }
       run_cli(&args).await
   }

   async fn run_cli(args: &[&str]) -> Result<serde_json::Value, String> {
       let output = tokio::process::Command::new("dataclaw")
           .args(args).output().await.map_err(|e| e.to_string())?;
       // Extract the JSON block after ---DATACLAW_JSON--- if present, else parse full stdout as JSON.
       parse_json_block(&output.stdout).map_err(|e| e.to_string())
   }
   ```
2. **Resolve** the `dataclaw` binary via (in order) `$DATACLAW_BIN`, `which dataclaw`, `~/.local/bin/dataclaw`, `/opt/homebrew/bin/dataclaw`, bundled Python (optional — leave behind a flag for later). If none found, surface an actionable install error in the UI.
3. **Register** commands in `main.rs` via `tauri::Builder::default().invoke_handler(tauri::generate_handler![dataclaw_status, dataclaw_config_get, dataclaw_auto_now, dataclaw_install_schedule, dataclaw_uninstall_schedule])`.
4. **Emit events** on auto-run completion by invoking `AppHandle::emit("auto-result", payload)` so the UI can toast "Pushed"/"Blocked".

### Step 25: Frontend — menubar panel
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Panel layout** (`app/src/routes/Dashboard.tsx`):
   - Header: `Last run: {last_auto_run.timestamp} — {pushed|blocked|noop}`
   - Stats: sessions/new findings/dataset URL
   - Primary button: **Run now** → `invoke("dataclaw_auto_now", {force: false})`
   - Secondary: **Open findings** → `Findings.tsx` showing `pf_new` with context snippets, with an "Add to redact list" quick-action that invokes `dataclaw config --redact "…"`.
2. **Config form** (`Config.tsx`): edit `repo`, `source`, `excluded_projects`, `redact_strings`, `redact_usernames`, `folder_rules.buckets`, `auto.policy`, schedule time. On save, invoke corresponding CLI subcommands.
3. **Menubar tray** (`main.rs`): `TrayIconBuilder` with menu items ["Open DataClaw", "Run now", "View last run", "-", "Quit"], left-click toggles the panel window.
4. **Use** `tauri-plugin-positioner` or compute window position from tray rect so panel hugs the menubar.

### Step 26: Schedule management via app (`app/src-tauri/src/schedule.rs`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **All scheduling goes through the CLI** (`dataclaw install-schedule --time HH:MM`, `dataclaw uninstall-schedule`, `dataclaw schedule-status`) — the Rust layer never writes the plist itself. Single source of truth.
2. **Add** `#[tauri::command]` wrappers `install_schedule(time: String)`, `uninstall_schedule()`, `schedule_status()` that forward to the CLI.

### Step 27: Packaging (`app/src-tauri/tauri.conf.json`, `app/package.json`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Configure** `bundle.targets = ["dmg", "app"]` and `bundle.macOS.signingIdentity = null` initially (user can sign later with their own cert).
2. **Add** `pnpm tauri build` build script; output lands at `app/src-tauri/target/release/bundle/dmg/DataClaw_*.dmg`.
3. **Do NOT** bundle the privacy-filter model — it downloads on first auto-run via HF hub. The DMG ships with the JS bundle + Rust binary only.
4. **Add** a top-level `Makefile` or `scripts/build-app.sh` wrapping: `cd app && pnpm install && pnpm tauri build`.

### Step 28: Tests — Tauri (`app/src-tauri/src/dataclaw.rs`, `app/src/lib/api.test.ts`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Rust unit test** for `parse_json_block` — feed a stdout with `"preamble\n---DATACLAW_JSON---\n{\"stage\":\"done\"}\n"`, assert parse returns the inner object.
2. **Rust unit test** for `run_cli` with a mock binary that returns fixed stdout via a `DATACLAW_BIN` env var pointing to a shell stub script in `tests/fixtures/`.
3. **Frontend** is intentionally under-tested in Phase 6 — visual smoke-test via `pnpm tauri dev` is the validation path (manual, `info`-priority success criterion).

---

## Execution Order (summary)

1. **Phase 1 first** — config schema + optional deps + cooled-session filter is the substrate. Low-risk, fully backward-compat; unblocks everything else.
2. **Phase 2** next — sharded HF upload can be validated end-to-end against a scratch HF dataset with a single synthetic session, independent of privacy-filter or auto-mode.
3. **Phase 3** (buckets) can proceed in parallel with Phase 2 once the path resolver scaffolding is in (Step 6).
4. **Phase 4** (privacy-filter) — heavy optional dep, ship behind feature flag (`config.privacy_filter.enabled`). Validate offline first with monkey-patched model before downloading the real one.
5. **Phase 5** (auto + scheduler) depends on 1–4; test the CLI exit codes and plist generation without actually installing launchd units in CI.
6. **Phase 6** (Tauri) last — it consumes the stable CLI contract. Start with read-only status view, then writable config form, then "run now", then tray, then DMG.

## Validation Order

1. **Unit tests per phase** — `pytest tests/test_config.py tests/test_parser.py tests/test_secrets.py tests/test_anonymizer.py tests/test_cli.py tests/test_privacy_filter.py tests/test_scheduler.py`.
2. **End-to-end dry run** (script — not committed): `dataclaw export --no-push --sharded -o /tmp/dc-e2e/` → inspect `/tmp/dc-e2e/claude/*.jsonl` and verify shard dates match `end_time`.
3. **HF smoke test** against a private scratch repo: push, then `load_dataset("user/scratch", "claude")` resolves shards.
4. **Auto-run smoke test**: `dataclaw enable-auto --publish-attestation "…"` → `dataclaw install-schedule --time 00:01` → wait 2 min → `dataclaw schedule-status` + inspect `~/.dataclaw/logs/auto.out.log`.
5. **Privacy-filter smoke test**: set `privacy_filter.enabled=true`, drop a session with a seeded fake PII string, run `dataclaw confirm`, verify it appears in `privacy_filter_new_findings` JSON.
6. **Tauri manual QA** (info-priority): `pnpm tauri dev`, click Run now, Config save, install schedule, verify tray icon behaves correctly, quit via menu item.

## Risk & tradeoff notes

- **Privacy-filter model size** (~1.5–3GB) — first auto-run after `enable-auto --enable-privacy-filter` will be slow. Mitigation: `enable-auto` warm-loads the model so the surprise is in setup, not in the cron window.
- **MPS OOM risk** with very long sessions — batch at 512-token windows; fall back to CPU on `torch.cuda.OutOfMemoryError` equivalent.
- **Launchd bootout/bootstrap** behavior varies across macOS versions — include both code paths and detect via `sw_vers -productVersion`.
- **HF `upload_folder` atomicity** — it's N files in one commit, but a mid-upload network failure leaves the repo partially updated. Strategy: upload to a temp `.dataclaw-staging/` path-in-repo first, then one more `upload_file` pointing at a `latest.json` manifest — we can skip this in v1 and accept the risk; add if users hit it.
- **Back-compat tests** — keep at least one test that runs `export --output conversations.jsonl` with no sharding, to lock the legacy path in.
- **Tauri codesigning** — unsigned DMGs get Gatekeeper-quarantined; document the `xattr -d com.apple.quarantine` workaround in README until the user gets a Developer ID.

    "Known-findings fingerprint = `sha256('{kind}|{text}')[:12]`, intentionally dropping offsets so the same name-hit across sessions collapses to one entry.",
    "Auto-mode exit codes: 0=success/noop, 2=missing deps or not enabled, 3=policy block, 4=HF push failure. Chosen so the scheduler and Tauri layer can distinguish the cases.",
    "launchd label: `io.dataclaw.auto`. Plist path: `~/Library/LaunchAgents/io.dataclaw.auto.plist`. Systemd unit names: `dataclaw-auto.{service,timer}` under `~/.config/systemd/user/`.",
    "HF upload uses `HfApi.upload_folder(..., allow_patterns=['**/*.jsonl'], ignore_patterns=['conversations.jsonl'])` \u2014 one commit per run. Not atomic; retries are best-effort.",
    "Back-compat: `dataclaw export --output path.jsonl` continues to write a single flat JSONL when `--sharded` is NOT passed; existing consumers are unaffected.",
    "Dataset README `configs:` block emits only configs for sources that actually have shards in this run (no empty-match configs, which HF rejects).",
    "Tauri app is a thin GUI \u2014 all writes go through the `dataclaw` CLI (including plist management). Rust never writes config or plists directly, only reads them for display.",
    "Tauri frontend is React + TypeScript + Vite (standard Tauri 2 template); swap to Svelte is ~4h of additional work if the user prefers.",
    "`enable-auto` requires `stage in {'confirmed', 'done'}` as a prereq \u2014 user has manually shipped at least once. Prevents blind automation of a fresh install.",
    "Notifications on blocked runs use `osascript` on macOS / `notify-send` on Linux; failures are swallowed so a missing notifier never fails the auto-run itself."
  ],
  "structure_warnings": []
}

You are already running inside the megaplan harness for this step. Do the requested planning/review/execution work directly. Do NOT invoke the `megaplan` CLI, do NOT read or activate the `megaplan` skill, do NOT start nested megaplan plans, and do NOT recurse into another planning harness.         Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/dataclaw





        Idea:
Plan a major expansion of the DataClaw project with five deliverables:

1. Periodic automatic runs — a `dataclaw auto` non-interactive command + one-time `dataclaw enable-auto` setup + `dataclaw install-schedule --time "HH:MM"` that writes a launchd plist on macOS (systemd timer on Linux). Replaces the human `confirm` gate with a policy check.

2. PII detection via the openai/privacy-filter model (1.5B total, 50M active MoE token classifier, ~750MB–3GB, runs on MPS). Augments — does not replace — the existing regex/entropy redaction in dataclaw/secrets.py and dataclaw/anonymizer.py. The new/known finding distinction drives the auto-mode policy.

3. Switch HF upload from overwriting a single `conversations.jsonl` to source-partitioned dated shards in a unified per-user repo: `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`. Track `last_export_cutoff` in config. Only export "cooled" sessions (source file mtime > 24h old). Declare HF `configs:` block in dataset README so `load_dataset(..., "claude")` etc. works. Leave existing root-level `conversations.jsonl` in place for back-compat and glob both.

4. User-defined folder organization: config entry `folder_rules: {buckets: {work: [...], oss: [...]}, default_bucket: "personal"}`. Projects map to buckets; shards land at `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`. Default bucket when unset: files stay at `{source}/...`.

5. Package as a MacBook app using Tauri — a thin GUI over the CLI that edits config, shows last run + findings, has a "run now" button, lives in the menu bar, and manages the launchd plist. Privacy-filter model downloads on first auto-run (not bundled in the .dmg).

Two open decisions the user has NOT yet answered — plan worker should call these out as explicit questions with recommendations:
- Auto-mode policy default: strict (any new person/secret detected by privacy-filter blocks push) vs permissive (auto-redact and push, surface findings after)
- Folder rules specification style: explicit per-project list vs tag-based (projects get tags, buckets defined by tag)

Current state of repo: Python CLI in dataclaw/{cli.py (~1638 lines), config.py, parser.py (~2038 lines), secrets.py, anonymizer.py}. Config at ~/.dataclaw/config.json. Sources supported: claude, codex, gemini, opencode, openclaw, kimi, custom. Existing commands: prep, list, config, export, confirm, status, update-skill. Existing confirm flow requires full name + 3 attestations + manual-scan-session count. Regex + entropy + anonymizer pipeline already handles bulk redaction. Uses pyproject.toml with optional deps. Tests exist in tests/.

Produce a sequenced implementation plan with phases, concrete file-level changes, tests, migration/back-compat concerns, and risk tradeoffs. User is technically strong — be specific rather than high-level. This is a code-mode megaplan (output is code, not a design doc).

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: DataClaw — Auto runs, privacy-filter PII, sharded HF uploads, folder rules, Tauri menubar app (v2)

## Overview

DataClaw today is a Python CLI that discovers coding-agent sessions (`claude`/`codex`/`gemini`/`opencode`/`openclaw`/`kimi`/`custom`), anonymizes paths & usernames (`dataclaw/anonymizer.py`), runs regex + entropy redaction (`dataclaw/secrets.py`), writes a single flat `dataclaw_conversations.jsonl`, and pushes `conversations.jsonl` + `metadata.json` + `README.md` to one HF dataset repo (`dataclaw/cli.py:409–456`). Config is a TypedDict at `~/.dataclaw/config.json` (`dataclaw/config.py:8–54`). The publish gate is `confirm` (`dataclaw/cli.py:941–1108`) — three text attestations + full-name scan + regex PII scan — which flips `stage="confirmed"` before `export` (`dataclaw/cli.py:1329–1595`) pushes.

This plan: (1) replaces the human `confirm` gate with a policy check so runs can happen automatically on a schedule; (2) augments regex redaction with the `openai/privacy-filter` MoE token classifier whose **new** findings drive the policy; (3) splits the single JSONL into `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` dated shards with an incremental cutoff and a manifest for directory-aware review; (4) lets the user group projects into buckets; (5) wraps it all in a Tauri menubar app that edits config, shows last run, and manages the launchd plist.

### Hard constraints (back-compat contract)
- **Function signatures preserved.** `export_to_jsonl(projects, output_path, anonymizer, include_thinking, custom_strings)` and `push_to_huggingface(jsonl_path, repo_id, meta)` keep their exact current call shapes — existing tests and any external importers continue to work. New behavior lives in **new functions**: `export_to_shards(projects, root_dir, config, anonymizer, ...)` and `push_shards_to_huggingface(root_dir, repo_id, manifest)`. The legacy functions gain a one-line delegation to the new path when a `DATACLAW_SHARDED=1` env var is set or when `config.auto.enabled`, but their default behavior is byte-identical.
- **Review path keeps working for one file AND a directory.** `confirm` auto-detects sharded exports via a manifest file at `{root_dir}/.dataclaw/manifest.json`; if present, it aggregates across shards. Otherwise it behaves as today.
- **Privacy-filter is opt-in.** Base `pip install dataclaw` must not import `torch`/`transformers`. Enforced by a dedicated test.
- **Tauri shells out only.** All writes go through the CLI. To make that work, the CLI gains the missing verbs (replace-list, remove-from-list, show-secrets).
- **User-facing copy stays in sync.** Every source enumeration, `next_steps` list, `SETUP_TO_PUBLISH_STEPS`, `EXPORT_REVIEW_PUBLISH_STEPS`, `AUTO_MODE_STEPS` (new), and help text lists the full canonical set `claude|codex|gemini|opencode|openclaw|kimi|custom|all`.

### Settled decisions (from gate, locked here)
- **Auto-mode default = strict** (new findings block push; user acks via `dataclaw confirm` or `--force`).
- **Folder rules primary form = explicit buckets**, with optional tag fallback; bucket matching normalizes project identity across sources.
- **Shard date = session end-time UTC date** (`end_time[:10]`); sessions with unparseable or missing `end_time` are skipped with a warning.
- **`last_export_cutoff` only advances on HF push success.**
- **Tauri: Rust never writes config or plists directly** — it invokes the CLI.
- **Optional extra name: `pii`** (opt-in for the OpenAI Privacy Filter model).
- **Cooled-session definition**: file mtime ≥ 24h for file-based sources; for opencode, latest-message-timestamp ≥ 24h. Covers **all** parser paths including Claude subagent-only directories.
- **Auto exit codes**: 0 success/noop, 2 missing deps or not enabled, 3 policy block, 4 HF push failure.

---

## Phase 1: Foundation — schema, optional deps, cooled filter, cutoff semantics

### Step 1: Extend `DataClawConfig` schema (`dataclaw/config.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** to the TypedDict at `dataclaw/config.py:12`:
   ```python
   last_export_cutoff: dict   # {source: ISO_end_timestamp_with_tz}
   folder_rules: dict         # {"buckets": {name: [project_key,...]}, "default_bucket": str|None, "buckets_by_tag": {name: [tag,...]}}
   project_tags: dict         # {project_key: [tag, ...]}
   auto: dict                 # {"enabled": bool, "policy": "strict"|"permissive", "publish_attestation": str, "full_name": str|None, "skip_full_name_scan": bool, "enabled_at": ISO, "dataclaw_binary": str, "schedule": {"time_hhmm": str, "installed_at": ISO}}
   known_findings: dict       # {fingerprint: {"kind": str, "preview": str, "first_seen": ISO, "times_seen": int}}
   last_auto_run: dict        # {"timestamp": ISO, "result": "pushed"|"blocked"|"noop"|"error", "new_findings": int, "sessions": int, "error": str|None, "repo_url": str|None}
   privacy_filter: dict       # {"enabled": bool, "model": "openai/privacy-filter", "device": "auto"|"mps"|"cpu", "min_score": float}
   ```

### Step 3: ISO-timestamp helper + cooled filter + `since` cutoff (`dataclaw/parser.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** near the source-constant block (`dataclaw/parser.py:23`):
   ```python
   import time
   from datetime import datetime, timezone

   COOL_DOWN_SECONDS = 24 * 60 * 60

   def _parse_iso(ts: str | None) -> datetime | None:
       """Robust parse of ISO-8601 timestamps from heterogeneous sources. None on bad input."""
       if not ts or not isinstance(ts, str):
           return None
       try:
           s = ts.rstrip("Z") + "+00:00" if ts.endswith("Z") else ts
           dt = datetime.fromisoformat(s)
           return dt if dt.tzinfo else dt.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)
       except (ValueError, TypeError):
           return None

   def _is_cooled_path(p: Path, now: float | None = None) -> bool:
       try:
           return (now or time.time()) - p.stat().st_mtime >= COOL_DOWN_SECONDS
       except OSError:
           return False

   def _is_cooled_ts(ts: str | None, now: float | None = None) -> bool:
       dt = _parse_iso(ts)
       return bool(dt and (now or time.time()) - dt.timestamp() >= COOL_DOWN_SECONDS)
   ```
2. **Thread** two new keyword-only params through `parse_project_sessions` (`dataclaw/parser.py:420–533`):
   ```python
   def parse_project_sessions(project_dir_name, anonymizer, include_thinking=True, source=CLAUDE_SOURCE, *, cooled_only: bool = False, since: dict[str, str] | None = None):
   ```
   Defaults preserve current behavior. Filter logic is **post-parse** so every source code path runs through it uniformly:
   ```python
   results = _dispatch_parse(...)  # existing
   if cooled_only or since:
       results = _apply_session_filters(results, source, cooled_only, since, source_files_by_session_id)
   return results
   ```
3. **`_apply_session_filters`** (new helper at the bottom of parser.py):
   - Drops sessions where `_parse_iso(session["end_time"])` is `None` — emit a single debug line per project to stderr: `"dataclaw: skipped N sessions with unparseable end_time under {project}"`.
   - If `cooled_only`: for file-based sources, drop if `_is_cooled_path(session["_source_file"])` is False; for SQLite-backed opencode, drop if `_is_cooled_ts(session["end_time"])` is False (uses end_time as last-activity proxy).
   - If `since`: drop where `_parse_iso(session["end_time"]) <= _parse_iso(since.get(source))` (strictly greater-than on the aware datetime, not string compare).
4. **Stamp** `_source_file` and `_project_dir_name` onto every session dict inside each parser (`_parse_claude_session_file`:682, `_parse_codex_session_file`:1259, `_parse_gemini_session_file`:898, `_parse_openclaw_session_file`:984, `_parse_kimi_session_file`:1671, `_parse_custom_sessions`:370, `_parse_opencode_session`:536). These are internal fields (prefixed `_`) stripped before JSONL write.
5. **Subagent coverage**: the Claude merge block at `dataclaw/parser.py:749–777` also stamps `_source_file` from the *latest* contributing JSONL in the merged set; cooled filter uses `max(mtimes)` to decide. Add a test covering this.

### Step 8: Bucket resolver with project-identity normalization (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** (new helper, after source-choice helpers ~`dataclaw/cli.py:62`):
   ```python
   def _project_candidates(session: dict) -> list[str]:
       """Possible keys a bucket rule might use. Order: dir_name > raw project > stripped prefix."""
       out = []
       if session.get("_project_dir_name"):
           out.append(session["_project_dir_name"])
       p = session.get("project") or ""
       if p:
           out.append(p)
           # codex/opencode/kimi/custom use "<source>:<basename>" display form
           if ":" in p:
               out.append(p.split(":", 1)[1])
       return out

   def _bucket_for_project(session: dict, config: DataClawConfig) -> str | None:
       rules = config.get("folder_rules") or {}
       buckets = rules.get("buckets") or {}
       candidates = _project_candidates(session)
       for bucket, members in buckets.items():
           if any(c in members for c in candidates):
               return bucket
       tags_map = config.get("project_tags") or {}
       tags_of_project = set()
       for c in candidates:
           tags_of_project.update(tags_map.get(c, []))
       if tags_of_project:
           for bucket, bucket_tags in (rules.get("buckets_by_tag") or {}).items():
               if tags_of_project.intersection(bucket_tags):
                   return bucket
       return rules.get("default_bucket")
   ```
2. **Document** in the help text for `--assign` (Step 14) that project keys can be either the discovery `dir_name` or the display-form `project` string.

## Phase 4: Privacy-filter PII detection

### Step 17: `dataclaw/privacy_filter.py` — decoder via HF pipeline (`dataclaw/privacy_filter.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** the module with lazy imports:
   ```python
   from __future__ import annotations
   import hashlib, sys
   from dataclasses import dataclass
   from pathlib import Path

   MODEL_ID = "openai/privacy-filter"
   _PIPE = None

   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class Finding:
       kind: str; text: str; start: int; end: int
       score: float; session_id: str; field: str
       def fingerprint(self) -> str:
           return hashlib.sha256(f"{self.kind}|{self.text}".encode()).hexdigest()[:12]

   def is_available() -> bool:
       try:
           import transformers, torch  # noqa: F401
           return True
       except ImportError:
           return False

   def _load(device: str = "auto"):
       """Load as a token-classification pipeline. The pipeline decodes the model's
       BIOES labels via aggregation_strategy='simple', which merges contiguous spans
       of the same entity type and computes per-span scores. This matches the model
       card's intended operating-point behavior closely enough for our new/known
       diffing purpose; exact-Viterbi reproduction is not needed because fingerprints
       are computed on final text spans, not on tag probabilities."""
       global _PIPE
       if _PIPE is not None: return _PIPE
       import torch
       from transformers import pipeline
       if device == "auto":
           device = "mps" if torch.backends.mps.is_available() else "cpu"
       print(f"dataclaw: loading {MODEL_ID} on {device} (first run downloads ~1.5GB)", file=sys.stderr)
       _PIPE = pipeline("token-classification", model=MODEL_ID, aggregation_strategy="simple", device=device)
       return _PIPE

   def scan_text(text: str, session_id: str, field: str, min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]:
       pipe = _load()
       # chunk at 512 tokens to respect MPS memory on long conversations
       findings = []
       for chunk_start, chunk in _chunk_by_tokens(text, pipe.tokenizer, max_tokens=480):
           for r in pipe(chunk):
               if r["score"] < min_score: continue
               findings.append(Finding(kind=r["entity_group"], text=r["word"],
                                       start=chunk_start + r["start"], end=chunk_start + r["end"],
                                       score=r["score"], session_id=session_id, field=field))
       return findings

   def scan_session(session: dict, min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]: ...
   def scan_shards(root_dir: Path, min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]: ...
   def scan_jsonl(path: Path, min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]: ...  # legacy single-file
   ```
2. **Decoder rationale (locked)**: Transformers' `pipeline(aggregation_strategy="simple")` handles BIOES/BIO label merging natively — using the pipeline is cheaper to maintain than writing our own Viterbi and still produces valid, contiguous spans tagged with the correct entity group. If the model card insists on strict Viterbi for score calibration, swap `aggregation_strategy` to `"first"` which still respects label transitions; there is no scenario in this plan where our fingerprint-based diffing requires Viterbi-exact decoding.
3. **Never mutate the session.** `scan_*` only returns `Finding` objects; redaction is a separate decision made by `confirm`/`auto`.

## Phase 5: Auto mode + OS scheduling with env preservation

### Step 21: `dataclaw enable-auto` (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** subparser:
   ```python
   ea = sub.add_parser("enable-auto", help="One-time setup for non-interactive auto-publish.")
   ea.add_argument("--publish-attestation", required=True)
   ea.add_argument("--policy", choices=["strict","permissive"], default="strict")
   ea.add_argument("--full-name", default=None)
   ea.add_argument("--skip-full-name-scan", action="store_true")
   ea.add_argument("--enable-privacy-filter", action="store_true")
   ```
2. **Handler** `enable_auto(args)`:
   - Requires `stage in {"confirmed","done"}` (user has shipped manually at least once).
   - Resolves the `dataclaw` binary via `shutil.which("dataclaw")` (fall back to `sys.executable -m dataclaw` if not on PATH) and records it in `config["auto"]["dataclaw_binary"]`.
   - If `--enable-privacy-filter`: set `config["privacy_filter"]["enabled"] = True` and import-test `dataclaw.privacy_filter`; fail with install hint if missing.
   - Save `config["auto"]` and emit JSON with `next_command: "dataclaw install-schedule --time 03:00"`.

### Step 22: `dataclaw auto` (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Subparser**: `auto` with `--force`, `--dry-run`, `--policy-override {strict,permissive}`.
2. **Handler** `auto(args)`:
   ```python
   config = load_config()
   if not (config.get("auto") or {}).get("enabled"):
       fail_json({"error":"Auto mode not enabled","fix":"dataclaw enable-auto --publish-attestation '…'"}, exit_code=2)
   policy = args.policy_override or config["auto"]["policy"]
   root = Path.home() / ".dataclaw" / "export"
   root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   manifest = export_to_shards(projects, root, anonymizer, config, cooled_only=True, since=config.get("last_export_cutoff", {}))
   if manifest["total_sessions"] == 0:
       record_last_auto_run("noop", config); save_config(config); return  # exit 0
   new_findings = []
   if config.get("privacy_filter",{}).get("enabled"):
       all_findings = pf.scan_shards(root)
       new_findings, seen = pf.diff_findings(all_findings, config.get("known_findings",{}))
       if policy == "strict" and new_findings and not args.force:
           record_last_auto_run("blocked", config, new=len(new_findings)); save_config(config)
           scheduler.notify("DataClaw blocked", f"{len(new_findings)} new findings; run `dataclaw confirm {root}`")
           sys.exit(3)
       if policy == "permissive":
           for f in new_findings:
               if f.text not in config.get("redact_strings",[]):
                   config.setdefault("redact_strings",[]).append(f.text)
           # re-run redaction on staging shards in place
           _reredact_shards_in_place(root, config["redact_strings"])
           config["known_findings"] = pf.record_findings(all_findings, config.get("known_findings",{}))
   if args.dry_run:
       record_last_auto_run("dry-run", config, sessions=manifest["total_sessions"]); save_config(config); return
   url = push_shards_to_huggingface(root, config["repo"], manifest)
   config["last_export_cutoff"] = {**config.get("last_export_cutoff",{}), **manifest["max_end_time_by_source"]}
   record_last_auto_run("pushed", config, sessions=manifest["total_sessions"], repo_url=url)
   save_config(config)
   ```
3. **Helper** `_evaluate_auto_policy(new_findings, policy, force) -> Literal["proceed","block"]` for isolated testability.

### Step 23: `dataclaw install-schedule` + env preservation (`dataclaw/scheduler.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **New module** `dataclaw/scheduler.py`:
   ```python
   PLIST_LABEL = "io.dataclaw.auto"
   SYSTEMD_UNIT = "dataclaw-auto"

   def _resolve_binary() -> str:
       from dataclaw.config import load_config
       cfg = load_config()
       b = (cfg.get("auto") or {}).get("dataclaw_binary")
       if b and Path(b).exists(): return b
       b = shutil.which("dataclaw")
       if b: return b
       return f"{sys.executable} -m dataclaw"  # space-split consumers must handle

   def _capture_env() -> dict[str, str]:
       keep = ("PATH","HOME","HF_HOME","HUGGINGFACE_HUB_CACHE","HF_TOKEN","LANG","LC_ALL","TMPDIR")
       return {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items() if k in keep}
   ```
2. **macOS `install(time_hhmm)`**:
   - Parse `HH:MM` → `Hour`, `Minute`.
   - Write plist with:
     - `Label = io.dataclaw.auto`
     - `ProgramArguments = [_resolve_binary(), "auto"]` (split on space if shim form).
     - `StartCalendarInterval = {"Hour": H, "Minute": M}`
     - `RunAtLoad = false`
     - `WorkingDirectory = $HOME`
     - `EnvironmentVariables = _capture_env()`
     - `StandardOutPath = ~/.dataclaw/logs/auto.out.log`
     - `StandardErrorPath = ~/.dataclaw/logs/auto.err.log`
   - Atomically write, then `launchctl bootout gui/$(id -u) <plist>` (ignore "not loaded") + `launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) <plist>`. Fall back to `launchctl unload/load` when `sw_vers` reports ≤ 10.14.
3. **Linux**:
   - `~/.config/systemd/user/dataclaw-auto.service`:
     ```ini
     [Service]
     Type=oneshot
     ExecStart=<binary> auto
     WorkingDirectory=%h
     Environment=PATH=<captured>
     Environment=HF_HOME=<captured>
     # ... one per captured var
     ```
   - `~/.config/systemd/user/dataclaw-auto.timer` with `OnCalendar=*-*-* HH:MM:00`.
   - `systemctl --user daemon-reload && systemctl --user enable --now dataclaw-auto.timer`.
4. **`uninstall` + `status`** match the install path: bootout + rm for macOS; `disable --now` + rm for systemd.
5. **Subparsers**: `install-schedule --time HH:MM`, `uninstall-schedule`, `schedule-status`. Save `config["auto"]["schedule"]` on success.

### Step 24: Notifications (`dataclaw/scheduler.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `notify(title, body)` using `osascript -e 'display notification …'` on macOS, `notify-send` on Linux; swallow failures.

### Step 25: Tests — auto + scheduler (`tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_scheduler.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`test_auto_requires_enable_auto`** — exit 2 when `auto.enabled` missing.
2. **`test_auto_noop_when_no_new_sessions`** — exit 0, manifest total_sessions==0, `last_auto_run.result="noop"`.
3. **`test_auto_blocks_in_strict_mode`** — monkey-patch `scan_shards` to return 1 new finding, strict → exit 3, no push call, notification fired.
4. **`test_auto_permissive_appends_to_redact_and_pushes`** — same setup, permissive → `redact_strings` updated, push called, cutoff advances.
5. **`test_auto_policy_override_flag`**, **`test_auto_dry_run_skips_push`**, **`test_auto_force_pushes_despite_findings`**.
6. **`test_install_schedule_macos_plist_contents`** — monkey-patch `sys.platform="darwin"` + `subprocess.run`; assert plist keys Hour/Minute/WorkingDirectory/EnvironmentVariables/ProgramArguments all present.
7. **`test_install_schedule_linux_systemd_unit_contents`** — similar for Linux.
8. **`test_capture_env_filters_to_allowlist`** — verifies the env-capture allowlist isn't leaking arbitrary vars.
9. **`test_cutoff_only_advances_on_push_success`** — push raises → `last_export_cutoff` unchanged.

## Phase 6: Tauri menubar app (thin GUI over CLI)

### Step 26: Scaffold Tauri 2 project (`app/`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Create `app/` with Tauri 2 + React + TypeScript + Vite (standard template). Structure:
   ```
   app/
     src-tauri/{tauri.conf.json, Cargo.toml, src/{main.rs, dataclaw.rs}}
     src/{App.tsx, routes/{Dashboard.tsx, Config.tsx, Findings.tsx}, lib/api.ts}
     package.json, vite.config.ts
   ```
2. `tauri.conf.json`: tray icon (template), `bundle.targets=["dmg","app"]` on macOS, `["appimage","deb"]` on Linux.

### Step 27: Rust backend — CLI bridge (`app/src-tauri/src/dataclaw.rs`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Binary resolution order**: `$DATACLAW_BIN` → `which dataclaw` → `~/.local/bin/dataclaw` → `/opt/homebrew/bin/dataclaw` → error tile in UI with install link.
2. **Commands** (consume the new CLI surface):
   - `dataclaw_status` → `dataclaw status`
   - `dataclaw_config_get(show_secrets: bool)` → `dataclaw config --show-secrets` (if true) else `dataclaw config`
   - `dataclaw_config_set_redact(values: Vec<String>)` → `dataclaw config --set-redact "..."`
   - `dataclaw_config_remove_redact(values: Vec<String>)` → `dataclaw config --remove-redact "..."`
   - same pairs for `redact_usernames`, `excluded_projects`
   - `dataclaw_config_assign(bucket, projects)` / `dataclaw_config_unassign`
   - `dataclaw_auto_now(force)` → `dataclaw auto` (+`--force`)
   - `dataclaw_install_schedule(time_hhmm)` / `dataclaw_uninstall_schedule` / `dataclaw_schedule_status`
3. **Output parsing**: look for `---DATACLAW_JSON---` sentinel in stdout; fall back to full stdout as JSON. Expose `parse_json_block(bytes) -> serde_json::Value`.

### Step 28: Frontend (`app/src/routes/*.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Dashboard**: last-run card (timestamp, result, sessions, repo URL), **Run now** button, **Open findings** button.
2. **Config**: form exposing repo, source, folder-rules buckets (drag-drop projects), redact lists (add/remove), redact-usernames (add/remove), excluded projects (add/remove), auto policy toggle, schedule time. All writes go through CLI commands from Step 27.
3. **Findings**: list `pf_new` with kind + preview + session; each row has "Add to redact list" (calls `dataclaw_config_set_redact` with union).
4. **Tray**: `TrayIconBuilder` with menu `[Open DataClaw, Run now, View last run, -, Quit]`; left-click toggles panel.

### Step 29: Packaging + tests (`app/`, `app/src-tauri/src/dataclaw.rs`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `pnpm tauri build` produces `.dmg` / `.AppImage`; privacy-filter model NOT bundled (downloads at first auto-run).
2. **Rust unit tests** for `parse_json_block` (preamble + sentinel + JSON) and for binary-resolution order (fake `$DATACLAW_BIN` pointing at a shell stub).
3. Frontend is info-priority; manual QA via `pnpm tauri dev` is the validation path.

---

## Execution Order

1. **Phase 1** first — schema, deps, cooled filter, ISO-timestamp parsing. Low-risk, backward-compat; unblocks everything.
2. **Phase 2** — sharded export with manifest + preserved legacy signatures. Validate the directory-aware confirm before wiring auto-mode to it.
3. **Phase 3** — bucket resolver + config CLI verbs + user-facing copy sync. Do the copy sync early so the Tauri consumption in Phase 6 isn't blocked.
4. **Phase 4** — privacy-filter behind the `pii` extra. Ship with pipeline-based decoding; defer Viterbi unless the model-card score calibration demands it.
5. **Phase 5** — auto + scheduler with env preservation. Test plist/unit generation without actually loading units in CI.
6. **Phase 6** — Tauri last, consumes the stable CLI contract.

## Validation Order

1. **Unit tests per phase** — `pytest tests/` (adds `test_import_isolation.py`, `test_privacy_filter.py`, `test_scheduler.py`).
2. **Legacy parity**: `test_export_to_jsonl_flat_path_unchanged_byte_for_byte`, `test_push_to_huggingface_legacy_signature_unchanged`, `test_confirm_on_single_file_unchanged`.
3. **Sharded E2E** (script, not committed): `dataclaw export --sharded -o /tmp/dc-shards/` → inspect tree + manifest → `dataclaw confirm /tmp/dc-shards/` → verify aggregated JSON.
4. **HF smoke**: push to a scratch repo, `load_dataset("user/scratch", "claude")` + `load_dataset("user/scratch")` both succeed.
5. **Auto smoke**: `enable-auto` → `install-schedule --time <2m-from-now>` → wait → inspect `~/.dataclaw/logs/auto.out.log` + `schedule-status`.
6. **Privacy-filter smoke**: seed a fake PII string in a session, toggle `privacy_filter.enabled=true`, run `confirm`, verify `privacy_filter_new_findings` surface.
7. **Tauri manual QA** (`info` priority): `pnpm tauri dev`, exercise Run now, Config save (replace + remove), Install schedule, tray behavior.

## Risk & tradeoff notes

- **Manifest-based confirm** keeps `confirm` backward-compat while enabling directory review. Trade-off: two code paths inside `confirm` — mitigated by extracting `_summarize_single_file` and `_summarize_shards` helpers sharing a result schema.
- **Pipeline-based decoding** (simple aggregation) may produce slightly different span boundaries than the model card's Viterbi. Fingerprints are computed on final text content, so the new/known diff remains stable across minor decoder differences. If calibration matters later, swap to `aggregation_strategy="first"` or a custom Viterbi helper without changing the public API.
- **Transformers floor (≥4.57)**: if the actual release shipping the `openai_privacy_filter` arch has a different number, bump in pyproject during the Phase 4 PR — verify by `pip install transformers==X.Y && python -c "from transformers import OpenaiPrivacyFilterForTokenClassification"`.
- **launchd env capture**: we copy only an allowlist of env vars (`PATH`, `HF_*`, `HOME`, `TMPDIR`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`) into the plist to avoid leaking secrets from the install shell into a persisted file readable by the user. If `HF_TOKEN` is set at install time, it's copied — same trust boundary as `~/.dataclaw/config.json`.
- **Config-CLI surface growth**: the new set-/remove-/show-secrets verbs add ~8 flags. Mitigation: group them visually in `--help` and document the Tauri-driven rationale.
- **Bucket normalization via `_project_dir_name`**: requires every parser to stamp that field. Test coverage ensures no source path is missed.

      "priority": "info",

      ]
    }
  ],
  "assumptions": [
    "Function signatures for `export_to_jsonl` and `push_to_huggingface` are preserved exactly as today; new sharded behavior lives in parallel functions `export_to_shards` and `push_shards_to_huggingface`. Existing tests and external importers continue to work untouched.",
    "Every sharded export writes a manifest at `{root}/.dataclaw/manifest.json`. `confirm` auto-detects sharded exports by looking for this file; single-file exports continue to be found by the existing `_find_export_file` logic. No breaking change to the confirm UX.",
    "Shard date uses `session.end_time[:10]` (UTC). Sessions with None or unparseable `end_time` are skipped with a single debug line per project. `_parse_iso` handles `Z` suffix and offset variants; it returns timezone-aware datetimes.",
    "`last_export_cutoff` comparison uses aware-datetime `>` (strictly greater), not ISO-string lexical compare. Only advances on successful HF push.",
    "Privacy-filter uses `transformers.pipeline('token-classification', aggregation_strategy='simple')` \u2014 relies on the pipeline's built-in BIOES/BIO label merging, not a hand-rolled Viterbi. Fingerprint-based new/known diffing is stable across minor decoder-calibration differences because fingerprints hash the final text span.",
    "`transformers>=4.57` is the floor (the release that shipped `OpenaiPrivacyFilterForTokenClassification`, per the model card dated 2026-04-22). The Phase 4 PR verifies the exact release number via `pip install transformers==X.Y && python -c 'from transformers import OpenaiPrivacyFilterForTokenClassification'` and bumps the floor if it differs.",
    "Base `pip install dataclaw` must not import `torch` or `transformers`. Enforced by `tests/test_import_isolation.py` running `python -c 'import sys, dataclaw.cli; assert torch/transformers not in sys.modules'` via subprocess.",
    "Config CLI gets replace and remove verbs (`--set-redact`, `--remove-redact`, etc.) plus a `--show-secrets` flag; these are required so the Tauri GUI can round-trip sensitive values. The existing append-mode `--redact` flag is preserved for script back-compat.",
    "Bucket rules match project identity via `_project_candidates(session)` which returns `[_project_dir_name, project, project.split(':',1)[1] if ':' in project else None]` \u2014 covers all source formats (claude/codex/gemini/opencode/openclaw/kimi/custom). Parsers stamp `_project_dir_name` as an internal field stripped before JSON write.",
    "Scheduler resolves the `dataclaw` binary via `shutil.which` at install-time and captures an env-var allowlist (`PATH`, `HF_HOME`, `HUGGINGFACE_HUB_CACHE`, `HF_TOKEN`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `TMPDIR`). Both launchd plist and systemd unit set `WorkingDirectory=$HOME`.",
    "Notifications use `osascript` on macOS and `notify-send` on Linux; notification failures never fail the auto-run.",
    "User-facing copy (constants + help text + next_steps) is synced in Phase 3, Step 15 to list the full `claude|codex|gemini|opencode|openclaw|kimi|custom|all` source set. A regression test guards it.",
    "Tauri remains a thin GUI: Rust invokes the CLI with the new set/remove/show-secrets verbs and the existing install-schedule/auto commands. It never writes config JSON or plists directly.",
    "`enable-auto` still requires `stage in {confirmed, done}` \u2014 at least one successful manual ship before automation is allowed.",
    "Auto-mode exit codes 0/2/3/4 remain as decided: success/noop, missing deps or not enabled, policy block, push failure."
  ],

      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: inspected `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/pod.py` and `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/ssh.py`. the existing `_build_ssh_client()` helper returns the package's own `runpod_lifecycle.ssh.sshclient` wrapper, not a raw `paramiko.sshclient`. that means step 0's promise that `pod.open_ssh_client() -> paramiko.sshclient` is a 3-line public wrapper around existing logic is not technically accurate, and step 20's `scp.scpclient(ssh.get_transport())` call would not work against the current wrapper type as written.",

        Idea:
Plan a major expansion of the DataClaw project with five deliverables:

1. Periodic automatic runs — a `dataclaw auto` non-interactive command + one-time `dataclaw enable-auto` setup + `dataclaw install-schedule --time "HH:MM"` that writes a launchd plist on macOS (systemd timer on Linux). Replaces the human `confirm` gate with a policy check.

2. PII detection via the openai/privacy-filter model (1.5B total, 50M active MoE token classifier, ~750MB–3GB, runs on MPS). Augments — does not replace — the existing regex/entropy redaction in dataclaw/secrets.py and dataclaw/anonymizer.py. The new/known finding distinction drives the auto-mode policy.

3. Switch HF upload from overwriting a single `conversations.jsonl` to source-partitioned dated shards in a unified per-user repo: `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`. Track `last_export_cutoff` in config. Only export "cooled" sessions (source file mtime > 24h old). Declare HF `configs:` block in dataset README so `load_dataset(..., "claude")` etc. works. Leave existing root-level `conversations.jsonl` in place for back-compat and glob both.

4. User-defined folder organization: config entry `folder_rules: {buckets: {work: [...], oss: [...]}, default_bucket: "personal"}`. Projects map to buckets; shards land at `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`. Default bucket when unset: files stay at `{source}/...`.

5. Package as a MacBook app using Tauri — a thin GUI over the CLI that edits config, shows last run + findings, has a "run now" button, lives in the menu bar, and manages the launchd plist. Privacy-filter model downloads on first auto-run (not bundled in the .dmg).

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: DataClaw — Auto runs, privacy-filter PII, sharded HF uploads, folder rules, Tauri menubar app (v3)

## Overview

DataClaw today is a Python CLI that discovers coding-agent sessions (`claude`/`codex`/`gemini`/`opencode`/`openclaw`/`kimi`/`custom`), anonymizes paths & usernames (`dataclaw/anonymizer.py`), runs regex + entropy redaction (`dataclaw/secrets.py`), writes a single flat `dataclaw_conversations.jsonl`, and pushes `conversations.jsonl` + `metadata.json` + `README.md` to one HF dataset repo (`dataclaw/cli.py:409–456`). Config is a TypedDict at `~/.dataclaw/config.json` (`dataclaw/config.py:8–54`). The publish gate is `confirm` (`dataclaw/cli.py:941–1108`).

This plan: (1) replaces the human `confirm` gate with a policy check so runs can happen automatically on a schedule; (2) augments regex redaction with the `openai/privacy-filter` MoE token classifier whose **new** findings drive the policy; (3) splits the single JSONL into `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` dated shards with per-session-id dedup, per-run staging, manifest-scoped uploads, and an incremental cutoff; (4) lets the user group projects into buckets; (5) wraps it all in a Tauri menubar app.

## User decisions (open — recommendations baked in; user must confirm or override)

**1. Auto-mode default policy — plan defaults to `strict`.** Any new privacy-filter finding blocks the push; user acks via `dataclaw confirm --ack-privacy-findings` or `dataclaw auto --force`. `permissive` (auto-redact + push + surface after) is available via `dataclaw enable-auto --policy permissive`. Rationale: publishing is irreversible and permissive silently launders hits the regex pipeline missed. **Open question: confirm strict, or override to permissive.**

**2. Folder-rules specification style — plan defaults to explicit buckets.** `folder_rules.buckets = {name: [project_key, ...]}` as primary; optional tag fallback via `project_tags` + `buckets_by_tag`. Rationale: explicit lists are a 30-second setup; tag-only forces a from-scratch taxonomy before anything works. **Open question: confirm explicit-first, or require tag-only.**

Both defaults are implemented so work can proceed. If either is overridden, only the default value and help text change — no architectural rework.

## Hard constraints (back-compat + correctness invariants)

- **Legacy function signatures preserved exactly.** `export_to_jsonl(projects, output_path, anonymizer, include_thinking, custom_strings)` and `push_to_huggingface(jsonl_path, repo_id, meta)` keep their current positional shapes — no caller breakage.
- **Sharded exports never overwrite prior sessions.** Same-date shards use per-session-id dedup on write, and when a remote shard already exists at the target path, it is fetched and merged before being rewritten.
- **Uploads are manifest-scoped.** `upload_folder` is invoked with `allow_patterns = [s["path"] for s in manifest["shards"]]` (exact relative paths), not a glob — stale files from prior runs cannot sneak in.
- **Per-run staging dirs**, not a persistent staging root. Each `export_to_shards` writes to `~/.dataclaw/export/runs/{export_id}/`. Blocked runs are preserved; successful runs are kept for a brief debugging window; older runs are garbage-collected.
- **`last_export_cutoff` advances only on push success.**
- **Privacy-filter is opt-in.** Base `pip install dataclaw` must not import `torch`/`transformers`; enforced by `tests/test_import_isolation.py`.
- **`confirm` is directory-aware.** Detects sharded exports via `{root}/.dataclaw/manifest.json`; single-file mode unchanged.
- **Tauri shells out only.** CLI gains `--set-<list>`, `--remove-<list>`, `--show-secrets` verbs so the GUI can round-trip values.
- **User-facing copy stays in sync** across the full `claude|codex|gemini|opencode|openclaw|kimi|custom|all` set.

## Settled decisions (from prior iterations — locked)

Auto default = strict. Buckets explicit-first with tag fallback. Shard date = session end-time UTC (`end_time[:10]`). Cooled = file mtime ≥ 24h (opencode: latest-msg ts ≥ 24h). Cutoff compares as aware datetimes with strict `>`. Legacy signatures preserved via new parallel functions. Manifest-based directory confirm. `pii` extra name. Pipeline-based decoder (see Step 17 rationale + Step 20 contract test). Env-allowlist for scheduler. Exit codes 0/2/3/4. Tauri is thin GUI.

### Step 3: ISO-timestamp helper + cooled filter + `since` cutoff (`dataclaw/parser.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** (near `dataclaw/parser.py:23`):
   ```python
   import time
   from datetime import datetime, timezone
   COOL_DOWN_SECONDS = 24 * 60 * 60

   def _parse_iso(ts: str | None) -> datetime | None:
       if not ts or not isinstance(ts, str): return None
       try:
           s = ts[:-1] + "+00:00" if ts.endswith("Z") else ts
           dt = datetime.fromisoformat(s)
           return dt if dt.tzinfo else dt.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)
       except (ValueError, TypeError):
           return None

   def _is_cooled_path(p: Path, now: float | None = None) -> bool:
       try: return (now or time.time()) - p.stat().st_mtime >= COOL_DOWN_SECONDS
       except OSError: return False

   def _is_cooled_ts(ts: str | None, now: float | None = None) -> bool:
       dt = _parse_iso(ts)
       return bool(dt and (now or time.time()) - dt.timestamp() >= COOL_DOWN_SECONDS)
   ```
2. **Thread** `cooled_only: bool = False`, `since: dict[str, str] | None = None` as keyword-only through `parse_project_sessions` (`dataclaw/parser.py:420`). Defaults preserve current behavior.
3. **Add** `_apply_session_filters(results, source, cooled_only, since)` — runs post-parse so every source path (including Claude subagent merges at `parser.py:749–777`) is covered uniformly. Logic:
   - Drop sessions where `_parse_iso(session["end_time"])` is `None` (one debug line per project to stderr).
   - If `cooled_only`: file-based sources → `_is_cooled_path(session["_source_file"])`; opencode → `_is_cooled_ts(session["end_time"])`.
   - If `since`: drop where `_parse_iso(session["end_time"]) <= _parse_iso(since.get(source))`.
4. **Stamp** `_source_file` and `_project_dir_name` (internal, `_`-prefixed, stripped before JSONL write) onto every session in each parser branch (`_parse_claude_session_file`:682, `_parse_codex_session_file`:1259, `_parse_gemini_session_file`:898, `_parse_openclaw_session_file`:984, `_parse_kimi_session_file`:1671, `_parse_custom_sessions`:370, `_parse_opencode_session`:536). Subagent merge stamps `_source_file` = the newest contributing JSONL (`max(mtimes)`).

## Phase 2: Sharded export — per-run staging + dedup + manifest-scoped upload

### Step 6: Path resolver + per-run `export_to_shards` (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `_resolve_shard_path(root: Path, session: dict, config) -> Path`:
   ```python
   day = session["end_time"][:10]
   bucket = _bucket_for_project(session, config)
   parts = ([bucket] if bucket else []) + [session["source"], f"{day}.jsonl"]
   return root.joinpath(*parts)
   ```
2. **Add** `export_to_shards(selected_projects, run_dir, anonymizer, config, *, include_thinking=True, custom_strings=None, cooled_only=False, since=None, fetch_existing=True) -> dict` with this contract:
   - `run_dir` is created fresh (caller passes a `~/.dataclaw/export/runs/{uuid4}/` path — see Step 22).
   - **Pre-flight: discover target shard paths.** First pass over sessions computes the set of unique `_resolve_shard_path(run_dir, s, config)` outputs without writing.
   - **If `fetch_existing` and `config.get("repo")`**: for each target path, compute the corresponding repo-relative path and attempt `hf_hub_download(repo_id, path_in_repo, local_dir=run_dir, local_dir_use_symlinks=False)`. On 404 / not-found, continue (first-ever push). On network error, raise and abort the run — do NOT proceed with partial merge.
   - **Open shard handles lazily**: for each target path, if the file exists locally (from the download), load existing `session_id` values into `seen_ids: dict[Path, set[str]]`. Open in `"a"` (append) mode so the existing content is preserved.
   - **Second pass over sessions**: skip if `session["session_id"] in seen_ids[path]`; else write JSON line, add to `seen_ids[path]`.
   - **Strip** internal `_source_file` / `_project_dir_name` before JSON write.
   - **Aggregate** per-shard stats and `max_end_time_by_source`.
   - **Write manifest atomically** (Step 7).
   - **Close all handles** in `finally`.

### Step 8: Bucket resolver with project-identity normalization (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** (after source helpers `~dataclaw/cli.py:62`):
   ```python
   def _project_candidates(session: dict) -> list[str]:
       out = []
       if session.get("_project_dir_name"): out.append(session["_project_dir_name"])
       p = session.get("project") or ""
       if p:
           out.append(p)
           if ":" in p: out.append(p.split(":", 1)[1])
       return out

   def _bucket_for_project(session, config) -> str | None:
       rules = config.get("folder_rules") or {}
       buckets = rules.get("buckets") or {}
       cands = _project_candidates(session)
       for b, members in buckets.items():
           if any(c in members for c in cands): return b
       tag_map = config.get("project_tags") or {}
       tags = set(); [tags.update(tag_map.get(c, [])) for c in cands]
       if tags:
           for b, btags in (rules.get("buckets_by_tag") or {}).items():
               if tags.intersection(btags): return b
       return rules.get("default_bucket")
   ```

### Step 9: Manifest-scoped HF push (`dataclaw/cli.py:409–456`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Leave** `push_to_huggingface(jsonl_path, repo_id, meta)` unchanged.
2. **Add** `push_shards_to_huggingface(run_dir: Path, repo_id: str, manifest: dict) -> str`:
   ```python
   api = HfApi()
   api.create_repo(repo_id, repo_type="dataset", exist_ok=True)

   # CRITICAL: scope the upload to ONLY the shards in this run's manifest.
   # This prevents stale files (from other run_dirs that happened to share
   # this path) or accidentally-included extras from being pushed.
   allow_patterns = [s["path"] for s in manifest["shards"]]
   if not allow_patterns:
       return f"https://huggingface.co/datasets/{repo_id}"  # noop

   api.upload_folder(
       folder_path=run_dir, path_in_repo="", repo_id=repo_id, repo_type="dataset",
       allow_patterns=allow_patterns,
       ignore_patterns=[".dataclaw/*", "conversations.jsonl"],
       commit_message=f"Update {len(allow_patterns)} shards ({manifest['total_sessions_new']} new sessions)",
   )
   api.upload_file(path_or_fileobj=json.dumps(manifest, indent=2).encode(),
                   path_in_repo="metadata.json", repo_id=repo_id, repo_type="dataset",
                   commit_message="Update metadata")
   api.upload_file(path_or_fileobj=_build_dataset_card_v2(repo_id, manifest).encode(),
                   path_in_repo="README.md", repo_id=repo_id, repo_type="dataset",
                   commit_message="Update dataset card")
   return f"https://huggingface.co/datasets/{repo_id}"
   ```
3. **Routing**: `_run_export` keeps calling the flat path by default; only `dataclaw auto` and the explicit `dataclaw export --sharded` flag route through `export_to_shards` + `push_shards_to_huggingface`.

### Step 10: README `configs:` block — `_build_dataset_card_v2` (`dataclaw/cli.py:459–576`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `_build_dataset_card_v2(repo_id, manifest)` adjacent to the original.
2. **Emit** YAML that unions flat file + shard tree; one `config_name: <source>` entry per source in `manifest["sources"]` (emit nothing for sources with zero shards — HF rejects empty-match configs):
   ```yaml
   configs:
     - config_name: default
       data_files:
         - split: train
           path: ["conversations.jsonl", "**/*.jsonl"]
     - config_name: claude
       data_files:
         - split: train
   ```
