## Phase 4: Privacy-filter PII detection

### Step 17: `dataclaw/privacy_filter.py` — pipeline decoder (`dataclaw/privacy_filter.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** the module (lazy imports, `device`/`min_score` as first-class params):
   ```python
   from __future__ import annotations
   import hashlib, sys
   from dataclasses import dataclass
   from pathlib import Path

   MODEL_ID = "openai/privacy-filter"
   _PIPE_CACHE: dict[str, object] = {}

   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class Finding:
       kind: str; text: str; start: int; end: int
       score: float; session_id: str; field: str
       def fingerprint(self) -> str:
           return hashlib.sha256(f"{self.kind}|{self.text}".encode()).hexdigest()[:12]

   def is_available() -> bool:
       try:
           import transformers, torch  # noqa
           return True
       except ImportError:
           return False

   def _load(device: str = "auto"):
       """Load as a token-classification pipeline. Transformers' pipeline with
       aggregation_strategy='simple' merges contiguous BIOES spans of the same
       entity type. Exact-Viterbi reproduction is NOT required for our new/known
       diffing because fingerprints hash final text spans, not tag probabilities.
       A contract test (Step 20) verifies pipeline output against canonical
       model-card example strings before we trust the decoder in production."""
       if device in _PIPE_CACHE: return _PIPE_CACHE[device]
       import torch
       from transformers import pipeline
       resolved = ("mps" if torch.backends.mps.is_available() else "cpu") if device == "auto" else device
       print(f"dataclaw: loading {MODEL_ID} on {resolved} (first run downloads ~1.5GB)", file=sys.stderr)
       pipe = pipeline("token-classification", model=MODEL_ID,
                       aggregation_strategy="simple", device=resolved)
       _PIPE_CACHE[device] = pipe
       return pipe

   def scan_text(text: str, session_id: str, field: str, *, device: str = "auto", min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]:
       pipe = _load(device)
       out = []
       for chunk_start, chunk in _chunk_by_tokens(text, pipe.tokenizer, max_tokens=480):
           for r in pipe(chunk):
               if r["score"] < min_score: continue
               out.append(Finding(kind=r["entity_group"], text=r["word"],
                                  start=chunk_start + r["start"], end=chunk_start + r["end"],
                                  score=float(r["score"]), session_id=session_id, field=field))
       return out

   def scan_session(session: dict, *, device: str = "auto", min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]: ...
   def scan_shards(root_dir: Path, *, device: str = "auto", min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]: ...
   def scan_jsonl(path: Path, *, device: str = "auto", min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]: ...
   ```
2. **Critical**: `device` and `min_score` are **keyword-only on every public entry point** so call sites are forced to pass them through — prevents config-value drift (Step 19 and Step 22 wire them).
3. **Never mutate sessions.** `scan_*` returns `Finding` objects only.

### Step 18: Known-findings registry (`dataclaw/privacy_filter.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `diff_findings(findings, known) -> (new, seen)` and `record_findings(findings, known) -> known` (fingerprint-keyed, O(1) lookup).

### Step 19: Wire into `confirm` — thread device + min_score explicitly (`dataclaw/cli.py:1033`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **At the existing PII scan call** (`cli.py:1034`):
   ```python
   config_pf = config.get("privacy_filter") or {}
   pf_new, pf_seen = [], []
   if config_pf.get("enabled"):
       from dataclaw import privacy_filter as pf
       if not pf.is_available():
           fail_json({"error": "privacy-filter requires optional deps",
                      "fix": "pip install 'dataclaw[pii]'"}, exit_code=2)
       device = config_pf.get("device", "auto")
       min_score = config_pf.get("min_score", 0.5)
       findings = (pf.scan_shards(root_dir, device=device, min_score=min_score)
                   if mode == "shards"
                   else pf.scan_jsonl(file_path, device=device, min_score=min_score))
       pf_new, pf_seen = pf.diff_findings(findings, config.get("known_findings", {}))
   ```
2. **Surface** `pf_new` and `pf_seen` in JSON output (separate keys).
3. **`confirm --policy strict`** (new flag, default `strict`) exits non-zero when `pf_new` is non-empty unless `--ack-privacy-findings` is passed. On ack: merge `pf_new` into `known_findings` and `save_config`.

### Step 20: Tests + contract test for decoder (`tests/test_privacy_filter.py` — new)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`test_fingerprint_deterministic`**, **`test_diff_findings_splits_correctly`**, **`test_record_findings_increments`**.
2. **`test_is_available_false_without_deps`** via `monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, "transformers", None)`.
3. **`test_scan_text_passes_device_and_min_score_through`** — monkey-patch `_load` with a spy; assert `device="mps"` and `min_score=0.3` from config reach the pipeline + filtering.

5. **Contract test `test_pipeline_decoder_matches_model_card_examples`** (marked `@pytest.mark.pii` so it runs only with the optional extra installed):
   - Loads the real model via `_load("cpu")`.
   - Runs a small set of canonical strings from the model card (e.g. `"My name is Jane Doe and my email is [REDACTED]"`).
   - Asserts expected entity kinds are present with scores above 0.5.
   - Purpose: behaviourally proves the pipeline decoder produces usable findings on real inputs, replacing speculative debate about BIOES-vs-Viterbi with an empirical gate. If this test ever fails on a minor Transformers release, the floor gets bumped and/or we swap `aggregation_strategy`.
   - Skipped in the default test run (CI without the extra); documented in CONTRIBUTING / Phase 4 preflight.

## Phase 5: Auto mode + OS scheduling with env preservation

### Step 22: `dataclaw enable-auto` + `auto` + per-run staging (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

**`enable-auto`** (subparser + handler):
1. **Subparser**:
   ```python
   ea = sub.add_parser("enable-auto", help="One-time setup for non-interactive auto-publish.")
   ea.add_argument("--publish-attestation", required=True)
   ea.add_argument("--policy", choices=["strict","permissive"], default="strict")
   ea.add_argument("--full-name", default=None)
   ea.add_argument("--skip-full-name-scan", action="store_true")
   ea.add_argument("--enable-privacy-filter", action="store_true")
   ```
2. **Handler** `enable_auto(args)`:
   - Requires `stage in {"confirmed","done"}`.
   - Resolves binary via `shutil.which("dataclaw")`, stores in `config["auto"]["dataclaw_binary"]`.
   - If `--enable-privacy-filter`: set `config["privacy_filter"]["enabled"] = True` and import-test `dataclaw.privacy_filter`; fail with install hint if missing.
   - Save `config["auto"]`; emit JSON with `next_command: "dataclaw install-schedule --time 03:00"`.

**`auto`** (subparser + handler + **per-run staging**):
1. **Subparser**: `auto --force --dry-run --policy-override {strict,permissive}`.
2. **Handler** `auto(args)` — per-run staging is the key correctness change:
   ```python
   config = load_config()
   if not (config.get("auto") or {}).get("enabled"):
       fail_json({"error":"Auto mode not enabled",
                  "fix":"dataclaw enable-auto --publish-attestation '…'"}, exit_code=2)
   policy = args.policy_override or config["auto"]["policy"]

   # Per-run staging dir — never reused, never persistent.
   runs_root = Path.home() / ".dataclaw" / "export" / "runs"
   runs_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   _cleanup_stale_runs(runs_root, keep=3)  # retain last 3 for debug
   run_dir = runs_root / uuid.uuid4().hex
   run_dir.mkdir()

   manifest = export_to_shards(projects, run_dir, anonymizer, config,
                               cooled_only=True,
                               since=config.get("last_export_cutoff", {}),
                               fetch_existing=True)

   if manifest["total_sessions_new"] == 0:
       record_last_auto_run("noop", config, staging_dir=str(run_dir))
       save_config(config)
       return

   pf_new = []
   cfg_pf = config.get("privacy_filter", {})
   if cfg_pf.get("enabled"):
       from dataclaw import privacy_filter as pf
       findings = pf.scan_shards(run_dir,
                                 device=cfg_pf.get("device", "auto"),
                                 min_score=cfg_pf.get("min_score", 0.5))
       pf_new, pf_seen = pf.diff_findings(findings, config.get("known_findings", {}))
       if policy == "strict" and pf_new and not args.force:
           record_last_auto_run("blocked", config, new_findings=len(pf_new),
                                staging_dir=str(run_dir))  # preserved for review
           save_config(config)
           scheduler.notify("DataClaw blocked",
                            f"{len(pf_new)} new findings; run `dataclaw confirm {run_dir}`")
           sys.exit(3)
       if policy == "permissive":
           for f in pf_new:
               if f.text not in config.get("redact_strings", []):
                   config.setdefault("redact_strings", []).append(f.text)
           _reredact_shards_in_place(run_dir, config["redact_strings"])
           config["known_findings"] = pf.record_findings(findings, config.get("known_findings", {}))

   if args.dry_run:
       record_last_auto_run("dry-run", config, sessions=manifest["total_sessions_new"],
                            staging_dir=str(run_dir)); save_config(config); return

   url = push_shards_to_huggingface(run_dir, config["repo"], manifest)
   config["last_export_cutoff"] = {**config.get("last_export_cutoff", {}),
                                   **manifest["max_end_time_by_source"]}
   record_last_auto_run("pushed", config, sessions=manifest["total_sessions_new"],
                        repo_url=url, staging_dir=str(run_dir))
   save_config(config)
   ```
3. **`_cleanup_stale_runs(root, keep)`** — sort dirs by mtime desc; `shutil.rmtree` anything past `keep`.
4. **`_evaluate_auto_policy(new_findings, policy, force)`** — isolated testable helper.

### Step 23: `dataclaw install-schedule` + env preservation (`dataclaw/scheduler.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **New module** `dataclaw/scheduler.py`:
   ```python
   PLIST_LABEL = "io.dataclaw.auto"
   SYSTEMD_UNIT = "dataclaw-auto"
   _ENV_ALLOWLIST = ("PATH","HOME","HF_HOME","HUGGINGFACE_HUB_CACHE","HF_TOKEN","LANG","LC_ALL","TMPDIR")

   def _resolve_binary() -> str:
       cfg = load_config()
       b = (cfg.get("auto") or {}).get("dataclaw_binary")
       if b and Path(b).exists(): return b
       b = shutil.which("dataclaw")
       return b if b else f"{sys.executable} -m dataclaw"

   def _capture_env() -> dict[str, str]:
       return {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items() if k in _ENV_ALLOWLIST}
   ```
2. **macOS `install(time_hhmm)`**: plist with `Label`, `ProgramArguments=[binary, "auto"]`, `StartCalendarInterval={"Hour":H,"Minute":M}`, `RunAtLoad=false`, `WorkingDirectory=$HOME`, `EnvironmentVariables=_capture_env()`, `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` at `~/.dataclaw/logs/auto.{out,err}.log`. Atomic write, then `launchctl bootout` (ignore "not loaded") + `launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) <plist>`. Fall back to `launchctl unload/load` if `sw_vers` reports ≤ 10.14.
3. **Linux**: write `.service` (`Type=oneshot`, `ExecStart=<binary> auto`, `WorkingDirectory=%h`, one `Environment=KEY=VALUE` line per allowlisted var) and `.timer` (`OnCalendar=*-*-* HH:MM:00`) under `~/.config/systemd/user/`. Then `systemctl --user daemon-reload && systemctl --user enable --now dataclaw-auto.timer`.
4. **`uninstall` + `status`** match the install path.
5. **Subparsers**: `install-schedule --time HH:MM`, `uninstall-schedule`, `schedule-status`. Save `config["auto"]["schedule"]` on success.

**Scope:** Small

---

## Execution Order

1. **Phase 1** — schema + deps + cooled filter + ISO parsing. Backward-compat; unblocks everything.
2. **Phase 2** — sharded export with per-run staging, dedup, and manifest-scoped upload. Validate same-day-merge behavior with a scratch HF repo before wiring auto-mode.
3. **Phase 3** — buckets + config-CLI verbs + copy sync. Do the copy sync now so Phase 6 isn't blocked.
4. **Phase 4** — privacy-filter behind `pii` extra; run the floor-verification (Step 21) before merging.
5. **Phase 5** — auto + scheduler.
6. **Phase 6** — Tauri last, consumes the stable CLI contract.

## Validation Order

1. **Unit tests per phase** — `pytest tests/`.
2. **Legacy parity** — `test_export_to_jsonl_flat_path_unchanged_byte_for_byte`, `test_push_to_huggingface_legacy_signature_unchanged`, `test_confirm_on_single_file_unchanged`.
3. **Same-day merge smoke** (script): two `dataclaw export --sharded` runs 5 minutes apart with the cooled filter disabled; assert the combined shard file contains all sessions from both runs with no duplicates.
4. **Stale-upload guard smoke** (script): leave a stale `.jsonl` in an earlier run dir; verify `push_shards_to_huggingface` (called on the *current* run dir) does NOT upload the stale path.
5. **Contract test** — `pytest -m pii tests/test_privacy_filter.py::test_pipeline_decoder_matches_model_card_examples` after installing `dataclaw[pii]`.
6. **HF smoke** — push to scratch repo; `load_dataset(repo, "claude")` + `load_dataset(repo)` both succeed.
7. **Auto smoke** — `enable-auto` → `install-schedule --time <2m-from-now>` → wait → inspect `~/.dataclaw/logs/auto.out.log`.
8. **Tauri manual QA** (`info`): `pnpm tauri dev`, exercise Run now, Config save (replace + remove), install schedule.

## Risk & tradeoff notes

- **Same-day merge via HF round-trip**: each run that writes to a shard path that already exists on HF performs one `hf_hub_download`. For a typical user with 5–15 source directories, this adds < 3 seconds per run. On network failure, the run aborts rather than silently drop prior sessions.
- **Per-run staging dir vs single persistent dir**: per-run isolates blast radius and lets blocked runs preserve their context for `dataclaw confirm <run_dir>` without contaminating the next run. Cost: GC keeps the 3 most recent runs.
- **Manifest-scoped upload**: explicit `allow_patterns` list of exact paths means stale files cannot be pushed even if they exist in the run dir. Trade-off: uploading 50+ shards adds ~50 strings to the commit's allow-patterns; well within HF Hub's limits.
- **Pipeline-based decoding (locked)**: swapped for hand-rolled Viterbi only if the canonical-inputs contract test (Step 20.5) fails on a minor Transformers release. Fingerprint-based diffing hashes final text spans, so minor decoder-calibration differences don't change the new/known set in practice.
- **Transformers floor**: preflight verification (Step 21) runs at PR time; floor will be bumped if `>=4.57` doesn't actually load the arch.
- **Staging env leaks**: `_ENV_ALLOWLIST` limits what lands in the plist/systemd unit. `HF_TOKEN`, if set, gets copied — this is the same trust boundary as `~/.dataclaw/config.json` (user's own home, 0600).

## File-change index

| File | Phases | Nature |
|------|--------|--------|
| `dataclaw/config.py` | 1 | schema extensions |
| `dataclaw/parser.py` | 1 | `_parse_iso`, `_is_cooled_*`, `_apply_session_filters`, field stamps |
| `dataclaw/cli.py` | 1,2,3,5 | `export_to_shards`, `push_shards_to_huggingface`, `_build_dataset_card_v2`, `_find_export_target`, directory-aware `confirm`, bucket resolver, config verbs (set/remove/show-secrets/assign/tag), `enable-auto`, `auto` with per-run staging, schedule subparsers, copy sync |
| `dataclaw/privacy_filter.py` | 4 | new (pipeline decoder, fingerprint, diff/record) |
| `dataclaw/scheduler.py` | 5 | new (plist + systemd + notify + binary/env resolution) |
| `dataclaw/secrets.py`, `dataclaw/anonymizer.py` | — | unchanged |

  "questions": [
    "Auto-mode default policy: plan defaults to `strict` (new privacy-filter findings block push; user acks or uses `--force`). Alternative is `permissive` (auto-redact findings inline, push, surface after). Recommendation: strict, because publishing is irreversible. Confirm, or override to permissive.",
    "Folder-rules specification style: plan defaults to explicit `folder_rules.buckets = {name: [project, ...]}` with optional tag fallback. Alternative is tag-only (every project must be tagged before any bucket works). Recommendation: explicit-first. Confirm, or override to tag-only.",
    "Privacy-filter decoder: plan uses `transformers.pipeline(aggregation_strategy='simple')` plus a contract test (Step 20.5) that verifies behavior on canonical model-card inputs. Model card describes constrained Viterbi. Contract test is the empirical gate. If the test passes on the target Transformers release, is this sufficient, or do you want a hand-rolled Viterbi decoder anyway for exact model-card parity?",

Phase 4 preflight (Step 21) runs `pip install 'transformers>=4.57' && python -c 'from transformers import OpenaiPrivacyFilterForTokenClassification' && python -c 'pipeline(...)'` and bumps the floor to the earliest release shipping the arch if 4.57 doesn't load it. Verified floor is recorded in the PR description.",
    "Base `pip install dataclaw` does not import `torch` or `transformers`; enforced by `tests/test_import_isolation.py` via subprocess.",
    "Config CLI gets `--set-redact` / `--remove-redact` / `--set-redact-usernames` / `--remove-redact-username` / `--set-excluded` / `--remove-excluded` / `--show-secrets` verbs plus bucket/tag set+clear verbs, so the Tauri GUI can round-trip sensitive list values.",
    "Bucket rules match project identity via `_project_candidates(session)` returning `[_project_dir_name, project, project.split(':',1)[1] if ':' in project else None]`. Every parser (claude / codex / gemini / opencode / openclaw / kimi / custom / subagent-merged) stamps `_project_dir_name` before the filter pass.",
    "Cooled-session filter runs post-parse in `_apply_session_filters` so all source branches including Claude subagent-merged sessions are covered uniformly. Subagent merges stamp `_source_file` as the newest contributing JSONL (`max(mtimes)`).",
    "ISO-timestamp comparison uses `_parse_iso` returning aware datetimes; `None` / unparseable end_times are skipped with one debug line per project; cutoff comparison is strictly `>` on aware-datetime values. Cutoff advances only on push success.",
    "Scheduler captures an env allowlist (`PATH`, `HOME`, `HF_HOME`, `HUGGINGFACE_HUB_CACHE`, `HF_TOKEN`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `TMPDIR`) into both the launchd plist's `EnvironmentVariables` and the systemd unit's `Environment=` lines. `WorkingDirectory=$HOME`. Binary resolved via `shutil.which` at install time.",
    "`dataclaw auto` exit codes: 0 success/noop/dry-run, 2 missing deps or not enabled, 3 policy block, 4 HF push failure. Blocked runs preserve their staging dir and fire a notification pointing at `dataclaw confirm <staging_dir>`.",
    "Tauri app is a thin GUI over the CLI: Rust never writes config or plists directly. Frontend = React + TypeScript + Vite (standard Tauri 2 template).",
    "Two open user decisions (auto-mode policy default, folder-rules spec style) are now surfaced explicitly in a 'User decisions (open)' section in the plan body with recommendations, not just in metadata."

Plan a major expansion of the DataClaw project with five deliverables:

1. Periodic automatic runs — a `dataclaw auto` non-interactive command + one-time `dataclaw enable-auto` setup + `dataclaw install-schedule --time "HH:MM"` that writes a launchd plist on macOS (systemd timer on Linux). Replaces the human `confirm` gate with a policy check.

2. PII detection via the openai/privacy-filter model (1.5B total, 50M active MoE token classifier, ~750MB–3GB, runs on MPS). Augments — does not replace — the existing regex/entropy redaction in dataclaw/secrets.py and dataclaw/anonymizer.py. The new/known finding distinction drives the auto-mode policy.

3. Switch HF upload from overwriting a single `conversations.jsonl` to source-partitioned dated shards in a unified per-user repo: `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`. Track `last_export_cutoff` in config. Only export "cooled" sessions (source file mtime > 24h old). Declare HF `configs:` block in dataset README so `load_dataset(..., "claude")` etc. works. Leave existing root-level `conversations.jsonl` in place for back-compat and glob both.

4. User-defined folder organization: config entry `folder_rules: {buckets: {work: [...], oss: [...]}, default_bucket: "personal"}`. Projects map to buckets; shards land at `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl`. Default bucket when unset: files stay at `{source}/...`.

5. Package as a MacBook app using Tauri — a thin GUI over the CLI that edits config, shows last run + findings, has a "run now" button, lives in the menu bar, and manages the launchd plist. Privacy-filter model downloads on first auto-run (not bundled in the .dmg).

Current state of repo: Python CLI in dataclaw/{cli.py (~1638 lines), config.py, parser.py (~2038 lines), secrets.py, anonymizer.py}. Config at ~/.dataclaw/config.json. Sources supported: claude, codex, gemini, opencode, openclaw, kimi, custom. Existing commands: prep, list, config, export, confirm, status, update-skill. Existing confirm flow requires full name + 3 attestations + manual-scan-session count. Regex + entropy + anonymizer pipeline already handles bulk redaction. Uses pyproject.toml with optional deps. Tests exist in tests/.

User notes and answers:
- Four additional MUST-level requirements to incorporate into the plan. These are load-bearing for a public Mac app:

(A) CODE SIGNING + NOTARIZATION — the .dmg must be signed with a Developer ID Application cert and notarized by Apple so Gatekeeper doesn't block first launch. The user is enrolling in the Apple Developer Program; this note adds the programmatic pieces.

1. Add docs/RELEASING.md documenting the one-time manual steps the maintainer must do themselves (they cannot be automated): enroll at developer.apple.com/programs/enroll, generate Developer ID Application certificate, generate App Store Connect API Key (.p8 file), note the Team ID and Issuer ID.

2. Configure tauri.conf.json with macOS.signingIdentity set from env var DATACLAW_SIGNING_IDENTITY, and macOS.entitlements pointing to a new file app/src-tauri/entitlements.plist with the minimum entitlements required for hardened runtime (com.apple.security.cs.disable-library-validation = false by default; add only if PyInstaller runtime needs it and document why).

3. Create .github/workflows/release.yml that triggers on git tag v*:
   - Builds on macos-14 (Apple Silicon) and macos-13 (Intel) runners
   - Imports the Developer ID cert from [REDACTED] (unwrapped with APPLE_DEVELOPER_ID_CERT_PASSWORD) into a temporary keychain
   - Sets env: APPLE_API_KEY (from APPLE_API_KEY_P8_BASE64), APPLE_API_KEY_ID, APPLE_API_ISSUER, APPLE_TEAM_ID, APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY
   - Runs pnpm tauri build
   - Tauri bundler handles signing + notarization + stapling automatically when those env vars are set
   - Uploads the signed .dmg (both architectures) to the GitHub release via gh release upload
   - Documents required GitHub repo secrets: APPLE_DEVELOPER_ID_CERT_P12_BASE64, APPLE_DEVELOPER_ID_CERT_PASSWORD, APPLE_API_KEY_P8_BASE64, APPLE_API_KEY_ID, APPLE_API_ISSUER, APPLE_TEAM_ID, APPLE_SIGNING_IDENTITY

4. Add make targets in a top-level Makefile or taskfile: 'make release-local' produces an unsigned .dmg for devs without the cert (for local testing); 'make release-signed' runs the same signing flow locally when the maintainer has the cert in their login Keychain and the API key file available. Use APPLE_API_KEY_PATH for the local case.

5. README must be explicit: 'Pre-built .dmg files from GitHub Releases are signed and notarized — double-click to install. Building from source requires your own Apple Developer Certificate; see docs/RELEASING.md.'

6. Test: Add .github/workflows/ci.yml or amend existing that does pnpm tauri build WITHOUT signing on every PR (DATACLAW_SIGNING_IDENTITY unset) and verifies the unsigned .app/.dmg artifacts exist. Don't block PR CI on signing (only release workflow signs).

(B) PUSH / NETWORK FAILURE RETRY WITH LOCAL PERSISTENCE — auto mode is a background daily job; network flakiness must not lose data.

1. Modify push_shards_to_huggingface in dataclaw/cli.py (Phase 2) to implement exponential backoff: 3 attempts with 30s → 2min → 10min delays on retriable errors (urllib3 exceptions, HTTPError 5xx, 429 with Retry-After honored). Non-retriable (401/403 auth, 400 bad request) fail immediately.

2. Shard writer (export_to_shards) writes to ~/.dataclaw/staging/{run_id}/{bucket}/{source}/{shard_name}.jsonl during the run. On successful push, move the staged shards to ~/.dataclaw/staging/published/{run_id}/ (or delete per config). On failed push, LEAVE them in staging — next auto run's cooled-session filter + cutoff will naturally re-include them (they were never published) so no data loss.

3. Auto mode tracks last_auto_run.push_attempts and last_auto_run.backoff_seconds_total in RUN_SUMMARY.json. If all attempts fail, exit code 4 (HF push failure) and log a structured error event with the final exception. Tauri Dashboard surfaces 'Last push failed — retry' button that re-invokes dataclaw auto --retry-only (new flag that skips re-parsing and re-runs push against staged shards from the last failed run_id).

4. If staging directory exceeds 5 GB (accumulated from failed runs), log a warning and surface in the Tauri UI. Add 'Clear staging' button in the app.

5. Test test_push_retry_backoff: mock HfApi.upload_file to raise ConnectionError on attempts 1-2 and succeed on 3; assert 3 attempts made, total wait time approx 150s (mocked with freezegun), final success logged.

(C) AUTO-UPDATES VIA tauri-plugin-updater — a distributed Mac app without an update channel ages badly.

1. Add tauri-plugin-updater = '2' to Cargo.toml and @tauri-apps/plugin-updater to package.json. Configure tauri.conf.json with updater section pointing to https://github.com/banodoco/dataclaw/releases/latest/download/latest.json.

2. Release workflow (A.3 above) additionally publishes latest.json containing the version, platforms, and URLs of the signed .dmg. Tauri updater signs this with an ed25519 signing key (separate from Apple cert); public key embedded in the app, private key in GitHub [REDACTED]

3. App checks for updates on launch and once a week while running. On update available: show non-blocking toast in-app 'Update available: v0.4.1 — click to install.' Download + verify signature + relaunch.

4. Do NOT auto-install without user click — respect user agency. Add a 'Check for updates' menu item in the tray menu.

5. Document the updater private-key generation in docs/RELEASING.md (one-time: pnpm tauri signer generate, store private key as GitHub secret + in a password manager, public key committed to tauri.conf.json).

6. Test test_updater_signature_verification: feed a tampered latest.json with wrong signature; app rejects the update. Use tauri-plugin-updater's test harness if available, otherwise a Rust unit test on the signature verification helper.

(D) EMERGENCY ROLLBACK — the whole project premise is user privacy; a 'oh no, I leaked something' button is load-bearing.

1. Add CLI verb: dataclaw rollback --commit <sha> [--repo <repo>] [--dry-run]. Uses HfApi.create_commit with operations that revert to the specified commit's tree. By default, operates on the configured repo; --repo overrides.

2. Add dataclaw rollback --list that prints the last N commits of the configured repo with timestamp, message, shard counts added. Piped to the Tauri app to render as a list.

3. Tauri Dashboard: 'Recent pushes' list with a red 'Revert' button next to each entry. Clicking opens a confirm modal with the commit details; confirm → dataclaw rollback --commit <sha>. Show success with 'Dataset reverted to {commit_message}. Affected shards: N files.'

4. The rollback log entry itself is structured: {event: 'rollback', initiator: 'tauri'|'cli', target_commit: sha, previous_commit: sha, reverted_files: [...]}. Tauri UI shows a banner 'Rolled back {timestamp}' until dismissed.

5. Document in README under Privacy & Redaction: 'If something slips through, DataClaw has a one-click rollback. You can revert your HF dataset to any previous commit from the app or via dataclaw rollback.'

6. Test test_rollback_reverts_to_commit: push two commits to a scratch repo, call rollback --commit <first_sha>, verify the file tree matches the first commit's state (checked via HfApi.list_repo_files + hash). test_rollback_list_shows_recent_commits.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════
CLUSTER 2: DETAILED STRUCTURED LOGS
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════

Comprehensive, structured, rotating logs that make failure post-mortems cheap.

1. New Phase 5 step 'Logging infrastructure' (before the scheduler step). Create dataclaw/logging.py with setup_logging(run_id: str, level='INFO') that configures stdlib logging. Format: JSON lines, one record per event. Fields: ts (ISO8601 UTC), level, logger, msg, run_id, phase (discover|parse|redact|privacy_filter|push|confirm|schedule), session_id (opt), project (opt), source (opt), extra (dict).

2. Log destination: ~/.dataclaw/logs/auto-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl (rotated daily, keep 30 days via a simple prune-on-startup or TimedRotatingFileHandler). Duplicate WARNING+ to stderr for CLI case; full INFO to file only.

3. Every phase emits: phase_start with input counts, phase_end with output counts + duration_ms + redactions_applied. Every session parsed emits session_parsed with msg_count, tokens, cooled_at. Every privacy-filter finding emits pf_finding with fingerprint, category, confidence, known (bool). Every HF upload emits hf_upload_start/hf_upload_end with file, shard path, repo, commit_sha. Errors emit error with exc_type, exc_msg, traceback.

4. New CLI flag: dataclaw status --logs tails the latest N lines of today's log and pretty-prints. dataclaw status --logs --run <id> filters to one run.

5. Tauri UI adds a 'Logs' tab (not just Findings): streams the current log file via a Rust watcher (notify crate) to the frontend, color-codes INFO/WARN/ERROR, has copy-to-clipboard. 'Show in Finder' button opens ~/.dataclaw/logs/.

6. launchd plist (scheduler step) already sets StandardOutPath/StandardErrorPath — keep that as coarse capture; the JSON log is the fine-grained one. Both must exist.

7. Every auto run writes a RUN_SUMMARY.json alongside the log: { run_id, started_at, ended_at, result: pushed|blocked|noop|error, sessions_exported, shards_written, findings: {new, seen, blocked_by}, policy, push: {repo, commit_sha, files} }. Tauri Dashboard reads latest RUN_SUMMARY.json for 'last run' card.

8. Tests: test_logging_emits_structured_json (run a minimal export, assert every log line parses as JSON with required fields); test_logging_rotates (monkeypatch today's date, force a rollover); test_run_summary_written (auto run writes RUN_SUMMARY.json with all required keys).

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════
CLUSTER 3: HUGGING FACE TOKEN VIA macOS KEYCHAIN
═══════════════════════════════════════════════════

The Tauri app must handle saving and supplying the HF token so users never touch huggingface-cli.

1. Add a 'Hugging Face' section to the Tauri Config tab: token input (password field, masked by default, 'show' toggle), 'Test' button calling whoami via CLI and displaying username, 'Clear token' button, status line ('Logged in as alice' / 'Not signed in').

2. Storage: macOS Keychain via the keyring crate (keyring = '2' in Cargo.toml) on macOS, Secret Service on Linux. Service name 'io.dataclaw.app', account 'hf_token'. NEVER write the token to ~/.dataclaw/config.json.

3. Token delivery to CLI: Tauri Rust layer reads token from Keychain just before spawning the sidecar, sets env HF_TOKEN on the child process. huggingface_hub auto-reads HF_TOKEN — no CLI changes required for the happy path. Document HF_TOKEN as the canonical env var in CLI help.

4. New CLI verbs (for terminal users): dataclaw hf login --token-stdin (reads one line from stdin, writes to ~/.cache/huggingface/token via huggingface_hub standard path), dataclaw hf logout, dataclaw hf whoami. Tauri app doesn't use these — it uses Keychain + HF_TOKEN — but they keep the CLI usable standalone.

5. First-run onboarding: if Tauri launches with no Keychain token, show blocking 'Welcome' modal with token field + link to https://huggingface.co/settings/tokens. Cannot dismiss until entered OR 'Skip — I'll add it later' (leaves app read-only, Run Now disabled).

6. Security:
   - Never log the token value (add a test: logs must scrub HF_TOKEN env)
   - Tauri IPC commands handling the token are allowlisted in tauri.conf.json
   - Test that Run Now over a configured scratch repo succeeds with only Keychain-stored token (no ~/.cache/huggingface/token written)

7. 'What's NOT in the .dmg' docs: the HF token is the ONE thing the user must provide, via the GUI, once. Everything else (dataclaw binary, Tauri runtime) is bundled; privacy-filter model downloads on first Run Now.

═══════════════════════════════════════════════════
All three treated as MUST-level. Cross-check the regenerated plan contains concrete steps for every bullet above. These were lost due to a megaplan concurrency bug (filed separately); re-injection is the recovery path.

        Plan:
        # Implementation Plan: DataClaw — Auto runs, privacy-filter PII, sharded HF uploads, folder rules, Tauri menubar app (v3)

## Overview

DataClaw today is a Python CLI that discovers coding-agent sessions (`claude`/`codex`/`gemini`/`opencode`/`openclaw`/`kimi`/`custom`), anonymizes paths & usernames (`dataclaw/anonymizer.py`), runs regex + entropy redaction (`dataclaw/secrets.py`), writes a single flat `dataclaw_conversations.jsonl`, and pushes `conversations.jsonl` + `metadata.json` + `README.md` to one HF dataset repo (`dataclaw/cli.py:409–456`). Config is a TypedDict at `~/.dataclaw/config.json` (`dataclaw/config.py:8–54`). The publish gate is `confirm` (`dataclaw/cli.py:941–1108`).

This plan: (1) replaces the human `confirm` gate with a policy check so runs can happen automatically on a schedule; (2) augments regex redaction with the `openai/privacy-filter` MoE token classifier whose **new** findings drive the policy; (3) splits the single JSONL into `{bucket}/{source}/YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl` dated shards with per-session-id dedup, per-run staging, manifest-scoped uploads, and an incremental cutoff; (4) lets the user group projects into buckets; (5) wraps it all in a Tauri menubar app.

## User decisions (open — recommendations baked in; user must confirm or override)

**1. Auto-mode default policy — plan defaults to `strict`.** Any new privacy-filter finding blocks the push; user acks via `dataclaw confirm --ack-privacy-findings` or `dataclaw auto --force`. `permissive` (auto-redact + push + surface after) is available via `dataclaw enable-auto --policy permissive`. Rationale: publishing is irreversible and permissive silently launders hits the regex pipeline missed. **Open question: confirm strict, or override to permissive.**

**2. Folder-rules specification style — plan defaults to explicit buckets.** `folder_rules.buckets = {name: [project_key, ...]}` as primary; optional tag fallback via `project_tags` + `buckets_by_tag`. Rationale: explicit lists are a 30-second setup; tag-only forces a from-scratch taxonomy before anything works. **Open question: confirm explicit-first, or require tag-only.**

Both defaults are implemented so work can proceed. If either is overridden, only the default value and help text change — no architectural rework.

## Hard constraints (back-compat + correctness invariants)

- **Legacy function signatures preserved exactly.** `export_to_jsonl(projects, output_path, anonymizer, include_thinking, custom_strings)` and `push_to_huggingface(jsonl_path, repo_id, meta)` keep their current positional shapes — no caller breakage.
- **Sharded exports never overwrite prior sessions.** Same-date shards use per-session-id dedup on write, and when a remote shard already exists at the target path, it is fetched and merged before being rewritten.
- **Uploads are manifest-scoped.** `upload_folder` is invoked with `allow_patterns = [s["path"] for s in manifest["shards"]]` (exact relative paths), not a glob — stale files from prior runs cannot sneak in.
- **Per-run staging dirs**, not a persistent staging root. Each `export_to_shards` writes to `~/.dataclaw/export/runs/{export_id}/`. Blocked runs are preserved; successful runs are kept for a brief debugging window; older runs are garbage-collected.
- **`last_export_cutoff` advances only on push success.**
- **Privacy-filter is opt-in.** Base `pip install dataclaw` must not import `torch`/`transformers`; enforced by `tests/test_import_isolation.py`.
- **`confirm` is directory-aware.** Detects sharded exports via `{root}/.dataclaw/manifest.json`; single-file mode unchanged.
- **Tauri shells out only.** CLI gains `--set-<list>`, `--remove-<list>`, `--show-secrets` verbs so the GUI can round-trip values.
- **User-facing copy stays in sync** across the full `claude|codex|gemini|opencode|openclaw|kimi|custom|all` set.

## Settled decisions (from prior iterations — locked)

Auto default = strict. Buckets explicit-first with tag fallback. Shard date = session end-time UTC (`end_time[:10]`). Cooled = file mtime ≥ 24h (opencode: latest-msg ts ≥ 24h). Cutoff compares as aware datetimes with strict `>`. Legacy signatures preserved via new parallel functions. Manifest-based directory confirm. `pii` extra name. Pipeline-based decoder (see Step 17 rationale + Step 20 contract test). Env-allowlist for scheduler. Exit codes 0/2/3/4. Tauri is thin GUI.

### Step 3: ISO-timestamp helper + cooled filter + `since` cutoff (`dataclaw/parser.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** (near `dataclaw/parser.py:23`):
   ```python
   import time
   from datetime import datetime, timezone
   COOL_DOWN_SECONDS = 24 * 60 * 60

   def _parse_iso(ts: str | None) -> datetime | None:
       if not ts or not isinstance(ts, str): return None
       try:
           s = ts[:-1] + "+00:00" if ts.endswith("Z") else ts
           dt = datetime.fromisoformat(s)
           return dt if dt.tzinfo else dt.replace(tzinfo=timezone.utc)
       except (ValueError, TypeError):
           return None

   def _is_cooled_path(p: Path, now: float | None = None) -> bool:
       try: return (now or time.time()) - p.stat().st_mtime >= COOL_DOWN_SECONDS
       except OSError: return False

   def _is_cooled_ts(ts: str | None, now: float | None = None) -> bool:
       dt = _parse_iso(ts)
       return bool(dt and (now or time.time()) - dt.timestamp() >= COOL_DOWN_SECONDS)
   ```
2. **Thread** `cooled_only: bool = False`, `since: dict[str, str] | None = None` as keyword-only through `parse_project_sessions` (`dataclaw/parser.py:420`). Defaults preserve current behavior.
3. **Add** `_apply_session_filters(results, source, cooled_only, since)` — runs post-parse so every source path (including Claude subagent merges at `parser.py:749–777`) is covered uniformly. Logic:
   - Drop sessions where `_parse_iso(session["end_time"])` is `None` (one debug line per project to stderr).
   - If `cooled_only`: file-based sources → `_is_cooled_path(session["_source_file"])`; opencode → `_is_cooled_ts(session["end_time"])`.
   - If `since`: drop where `_parse_iso(session["end_time"]) <= _parse_iso(since.get(source))`.
4. **Stamp** `_source_file` and `_project_dir_name` (internal, `_`-prefixed, stripped before JSONL write) onto every session in each parser branch (`_parse_claude_session_file`:682, `_parse_codex_session_file`:1259, `_parse_gemini_session_file`:898, `_parse_openclaw_session_file`:984, `_parse_kimi_session_file`:1671, `_parse_custom_sessions`:370, `_parse_opencode_session`:536). Subagent merge stamps `_source_file` = the newest contributing JSONL (`max(mtimes)`).

## Phase 2: Sharded export — per-run staging + dedup + manifest-scoped upload

### Step 6: Path resolver + per-run `export_to_shards` (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** `_resolve_shard_path(root: Path, session: dict, config) -> Path`:
   ```python
   day = session["end_time"][:10]
   bucket = _bucket_for_project(session, config)
   parts = ([bucket] if bucket else []) + [session["source"], f"{day}.jsonl"]
   return root.joinpath(*parts)
   ```
2. **Add** `export_to_shards(selected_projects, run_dir, anonymizer, config, *, include_thinking=True, custom_strings=None, cooled_only=False, since=None, fetch_existing=True) -> dict` with this contract:
   - `run_dir` is created fresh (caller passes a `~/.dataclaw/export/runs/{uuid4}/` path — see Step 22).
   - **Pre-flight: discover target shard paths.** First pass over sessions computes the set of unique `_resolve_shard_path(run_dir, s, config)` outputs without writing.
   - **If `fetch_existing` and `config.get("repo")`**: for each target path, compute the corresponding repo-relative path and attempt `hf_hub_download(repo_id, path_in_repo, local_dir=run_dir, local_dir_use_symlinks=False)`. On 404 / not-found, continue (first-ever push). On network error, raise and abort the run — do NOT proceed with partial merge.
   - **Open shard handles lazily**: for each target path, if the file exists locally (from the download), load existing `session_id` values into `seen_ids: dict[Path, set[str]]`. Open in `"a"` (append) mode so the existing content is preserved.
   - **Second pass over sessions**: skip if `session["session_id"] in seen_ids[path]`; else write JSON line, add to `seen_ids[path]`.
   - **Strip** internal `_source_file` / `_project_dir_name` before JSON write.
   - **Aggregate** per-shard stats and `max_end_time_by_source`.
   - **Write manifest atomically** (Step 7).
   - **Close all handles** in `finally`.
3. **Do not touch** `export_to_jsonl` — legacy path remains byte-identical.

### Step 8: Bucket resolver with project-identity normalization (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Add** (after source helpers `~dataclaw/cli.py:62`):
   ```python
   def _project_candidates(session: dict) -> list[str]:
       out = []
       if session.get("_project_dir_name"): out.append(session["_project_dir_name"])
       p = session.get("project") or ""
       if p:
           out.append(p)
           if ":" in p: out.append(p.split(":", 1)[1])
       return out

   def _bucket_for_project(session, config) -> str | None:
       rules = config.get("folder_rules") or {}
       buckets = rules.get("buckets") or {}
       cands = _project_candidates(session)
       for b, members in buckets.items():
           if any(c in members for c in cands): return b
       tag_map = config.get("project_tags") or {}
       tags = set(); [tags.update(tag_map.get(c, [])) for c in cands]
       if tags:
           for b, btags in (rules.get("buckets_by_tag") or {}).items():
               if tags.intersection(btags): return b
       return rules.get("default_bucket")
   ```

### Step 9: Manifest-scoped HF push (`dataclaw/cli.py:409–456`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Leave** `push_to_huggingface(jsonl_path, repo_id, meta)` unchanged.
2. **Add** `push_shards_to_huggingface(run_dir: Path, repo_id: str, manifest: dict) -> str`:
   ```python
   api = HfApi()
   api.create_repo(repo_id, repo_type="dataset", exist_ok=True)

   # CRITICAL: scope the upload to ONLY the shards in this run's manifest.
   # This prevents stale files (from other run_dirs that happened to share
   # this path) or accidentally-included extras from being pushed.
   allow_patterns = [s["path"] for s in manifest["shards"]]
   if not allow_patterns:
       return f"https://huggingface.co/datasets/{repo_id}"  # noop

   api.upload_folder(
       folder_path=run_dir, path_in_repo="", repo_id=repo_id, repo_type="dataset",
       allow_patterns=allow_patterns,
       ignore_patterns=[".dataclaw/*", "conversations.jsonl"],
       commit_message=f"Update {len(allow_patterns)} shards ({manifest['total_sessions_new']} new sessions)",
   )
   api.upload_file(path_or_fileobj=json.dumps(manifest, indent=2).encode(),
                   path_in_repo="metadata.json", repo_id=repo_id, repo_type="dataset",
                   commit_message="Update metadata")
   api.upload_file(path_or_fileobj=_build_dataset_card_v2(repo_id, manifest).encode(),
                   path_in_repo="README.md", repo_id=repo_id, repo_type="dataset",
                   commit_message="Update dataset card")
   return f"https://huggingface.co/datasets/{repo_id}"
   ```
3. **Routing**: `_run_export` keeps calling the flat path by default; only `dataclaw auto` and the explicit `dataclaw export --sharded` flag route through `export_to_shards` + `push_shards_to_huggingface`.

## Phase 4: Privacy-filter PII detection

### Step 17: `dataclaw/privacy_filter.py` — pipeline decoder (`dataclaw/privacy_filter.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Create** the module (lazy imports, `device`/`min_score` as first-class params):
   ```python
   from __future__ import annotations
   import hashlib, sys
   from dataclasses import dataclass
   from pathlib import Path

   MODEL_ID = "openai/privacy-filter"
   _PIPE_CACHE: dict[str, object] = {}

   @dataclass(frozen=True)
   class Finding:
       kind: str; text: str; start: int; end: int
       score: float; session_id: str; field: str
       def fingerprint(self) -> str:
           return hashlib.sha256(f"{self.kind}|{self.text}".encode()).hexdigest()[:12]

   def is_available() -> bool:
       try:
           import transformers, torch  # noqa
           return True
       except ImportError:
           return False

   def _load(device: str = "auto"):
       """Load as a token-classification pipeline. Transformers' pipeline with
       aggregation_strategy='simple' merges contiguous BIOES spans of the same
       entity type. Exact-Viterbi reproduction is NOT required for our new/known
       diffing because fingerprints hash final text spans, not tag probabilities.
       A contract test (Step 20) verifies pipeline output against canonical
       model-card example strings before we trust the decoder in production."""
       if device in _PIPE_CACHE: return _PIPE_CACHE[device]
       import torch
       from transformers import pipeline
       resolved = ("mps" if torch.backends.mps.is_available() else "cpu") if device == "auto" else device
       print(f"dataclaw: loading {MODEL_ID} on {resolved} (first run downloads ~1.5GB)", file=sys.stderr)
       pipe = pipeline("token-classification", model=MODEL_ID,
                       aggregation_strategy="simple", device=resolved)
       _PIPE_CACHE[device] = pipe
       return pipe

   def scan_text(text: str, session_id: str, field: str, *, device: str = "auto", min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]:
       pipe = _load(device)
       out = []
       for chunk_start, chunk in _chunk_by_tokens(text, pipe.tokenizer, max_tokens=480):
           for r in pipe(chunk):
               if r["score"] < min_score: continue
               out.append(Finding(kind=r["entity_group"], text=r["word"],
                                  start=chunk_start + r["start"], end=chunk_start + r["end"],
                                  score=float(r["score"]), session_id=session_id, field=field))
       return out

   def scan_session(session: dict, *, device: str = "auto", min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]: ...
   def scan_shards(root_dir: Path, *, device: str = "auto", min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]: ...
   def scan_jsonl(path: Path, *, device: str = "auto", min_score: float = 0.5) -> list[Finding]: ...
   ```
2. **Critical**: `device` and `min_score` are **keyword-only on every public entry point** so call sites are forced to pass them through — prevents config-value drift (Step 19 and Step 22 wire them).
3. **Never mutate sessions.** `scan_*` returns `Finding` objects only.

### Step 18: Known-findings registry (`dataclaw/privacy_filter.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Add** `diff_findings(findings, known) -> (new, seen)` and `record_findings(findings, known) -> known` (fingerprint-keyed, O(1) lookup).

### Step 19: Wire into `confirm` — thread device + min_score explicitly (`dataclaw/cli.py:1033`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **At the existing PII scan call** (`cli.py:1034`):
   ```python
   config_pf = config.get("privacy_filter") or {}
   pf_new, pf_seen = [], []
   if config_pf.get("enabled"):
       from dataclaw import privacy_filter as pf
       if not pf.is_available():
           fail_json({"error": "privacy-filter requires optional deps",
                      "fix": "pip install 'dataclaw[pii]'"}, exit_code=2)
       device = config_pf.get("device", "auto")
       min_score = config_pf.get("min_score", 0.5)
       findings = (pf.scan_shards(root_dir, device=device, min_score=min_score)
                   if mode == "shards"
                   else pf.scan_jsonl(file_path, device=device, min_score=min_score))
       pf_new, pf_seen = pf.diff_findings(findings, config.get("known_findings", {}))
   ```
2. **Surface** `pf_new` and `pf_seen` in JSON output (separate keys).
3. **`confirm --policy strict`** (new flag, default `strict`) exits non-zero when `pf_new` is non-empty unless `--ack-privacy-findings` is passed. On ack: merge `pf_new` into `known_findings` and `save_config`.

### Step 20: Tests + contract test for decoder (`tests/test_privacy_filter.py` — new)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`test_fingerprint_deterministic`**, **`test_diff_findings_splits_correctly`**, **`test_record_findings_increments`**.
2. **`test_is_available_false_without_deps`** via `monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, "transformers", None)`.
3. **`test_scan_text_passes_device_and_min_score_through`** — monkey-patch `_load` with a spy; assert `device="mps"` and `min_score=0.3` from config reach the pipeline + filtering.
4. **`test_confirm_blocks_on_new_pf_findings_strict`**, **`test_confirm_ack_adds_to_known_findings`**.
5. **Contract test `test_pipeline_decoder_matches_model_card_examples`** (marked `@pytest.mark.pii` so it runs only with the optional extra installed):
   - Loads the real model via `_load("cpu")`.
   - Runs a small set of canonical strings from the model card (e.g. `"My name is Jane Doe and my email is [REDACTED]"`).
   - Asserts expected entity kinds are present with scores above 0.5.
   - Purpose: behaviourally proves the pipeline decoder produces usable findings on real inputs, replacing speculative debate about BIOES-vs-Viterbi with an empirical gate. If this test ever fails on a minor Transformers release, the floor gets bumped and/or we swap `aggregation_strategy`.
   - Skipped in the default test run (CI without the extra); documented in CONTRIBUTING / Phase 4 preflight.

### Step 21: Phase-4 preflight — verify Transformers floor (`pyproject.toml`, manual step)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Before merging the Phase 4 PR**, run a one-line verification to confirm the floor is correct:
   ```bash
   pip install 'transformers>=4.57' && \
     python -c "from transformers import OpenaiPrivacyFilterForTokenClassification" && \
     python -c "from transformers import pipeline; pipeline('token-classification', model='openai/privacy-filter', aggregation_strategy='simple')"
   ```
   If the import fails on 4.57, bump the floor to the earliest release that includes the arch. Record the verified floor in the `[project.optional-dependencies]` `pii` line and in the PR description.
2. **Add** a `CHANGELOG.md` (or PR-body note) entry: `"Verified openai/privacy-filter loads on transformers==X.Y"`.

## Phase 5: Auto mode + OS scheduling with env preservation

### Step 22: `dataclaw enable-auto` + `auto` + per-run staging (`dataclaw/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium

**`enable-auto`** (subparser + handler):
1. **Subparser**:
   ```python
   ea = sub.add_parser("enable-auto", help="One-time setup for non-interactive auto-publish.")
   ea.add_argument("--publish-attestation", required=True)
   ea.add_argument("--policy", choices=["strict","permissive"], default="strict")
   ea.add_argument("--full-name", default=None)
   ea.add_argument("--skip-full-name-scan", action="store_true")
   ea.add_argument("--enable-privacy-filter", action="store_true")
   ```
2. **Handler** `enable_auto(args)`:
   - Requires `stage in {"confirmed","done"}`.
   - Resolves binary via `shutil.which("dataclaw")`, stores in `config["auto"]["dataclaw_binary"]`.
   - If `--enable-privacy-filter`: set `config["privacy_filter"]["enabled"] = True` and import-test `dataclaw.privacy_filter`; fail with install hint if missing.
   - Save `config["auto"]`; emit JSON with `next_command: "dataclaw install-schedule --time 03:00"`.

**`auto`** (subparser + handler + **per-run staging**):
1. **Subparser**: `auto --force --dry-run --policy-override {strict,permissive}`.
2. **Handler** `auto(args)` — per-run staging is the key correctness change:
   ```python
   config = load_config()
   if not (config.get("auto") or {}).get("enabled"):
       fail_json({"error":"Auto mode not enabled",
                  "fix":"dataclaw enable-auto --publish-attestation '…'"}, exit_code=2)
   policy = args.policy_override or config["auto"]["policy"]

   # Per-run staging dir — never reused, never persistent.
   runs_root = Path.home() / ".dataclaw" / "export" / "runs"
   runs_root.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   _cleanup_stale_runs(runs_root, keep=3)  # retain last 3 for debug
   run_dir = runs_root / uuid.uuid4().hex
   run_dir.mkdir()

   manifest = export_to_shards(projects, run_dir, anonymizer, config,
                               cooled_only=True,
                               since=config.get("last_export_cutoff", {}),
                               fetch_existing=True)

   if manifest["total_sessions_new"] == 0:
       record_last_auto_run("noop", config, staging_dir=str(run_dir))
       save_config(config)
       return

   pf_new = []
   cfg_pf = config.get("privacy_filter", {})
   if cfg_pf.get("enabled"):
       from dataclaw import privacy_filter as pf
       findings = pf.scan_shards(run_dir,
                                 device=cfg_pf.get("device", "auto"),
                                 min_score=cfg_pf.get("min_score", 0.5))
       pf_new, pf_seen = pf.diff_findings(findings, config.get("known_findings", {}))
       if policy == "strict" and pf_new and not args.force:
           record_last_auto_run("blocked", config, new_findings=len(pf_new),
                                staging_dir=str(run_dir))  # preserved for review
           save_config(config)
           scheduler.notify("DataClaw blocked",
                            f"{len(pf_new)} new findings; run `dataclaw confirm {run_dir}`")
           sys.exit(3)
       if policy == "permissive":
           for f in pf_new:
               if f.text not in config.get("redact_strings", []):
                   config.setdefault("redact_strings", []).append(f.text)
           _reredact_shards_in_place(run_dir, config["redact_strings"])
           config["known_findings"] = pf.record_findings(findings, config.get("known_findings", {}))

   if args.dry_run:
       record_last_auto_run("dry-run", config, sessions=manifest["total_sessions_new"],
                            staging_dir=str(run_dir)); save_config(config); return

   url = push_shards_to_huggingface(run_dir, config["repo"], manifest)
   config["last_export_cutoff"] = {**config.get("last_export_cutoff", {}),
                                   **manifest["max_end_time_by_source"]}
   record_last_auto_run("pushed", config, sessions=manifest["total_sessions_new"],
                        repo_url=url, staging_dir=str(run_dir))
   save_config(config)
   ```
3. **`_cleanup_stale_runs(root, keep)`** — sort dirs by mtime desc; `shutil.rmtree` anything past `keep`.
4. **`_evaluate_auto_policy(new_findings, policy, force)`** — isolated testable helper.

### Step 23: `dataclaw install-schedule` + env preservation (`dataclaw/scheduler.py` — new)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **New module** `dataclaw/scheduler.py`:
   ```python
   PLIST_LABEL = "io.dataclaw.auto"
   SYSTEMD_UNIT = "dataclaw-auto"
   _ENV_ALLOWLIST = ("PATH","HOME","HF_HOME","HUGGINGFACE_HUB_CACHE","HF_TOKEN","LANG","LC_ALL","TMPDIR")

   def _resolve_binary() -> str:
       cfg = load_config()
       b = (cfg.get("auto") or {}).get("dataclaw_binary")
       if b and Path(b).exists(): return b
       b = shutil.which("dataclaw")
       return b if b else f"{sys.executable} -m dataclaw"

   def _capture_env() -> dict[str, str]:
       return {k: v for k, v in os.environ.items() if k in _ENV_ALLOWLIST}
   ```
2. **macOS `install(time_hhmm)`**: plist with `Label`, `ProgramArguments=[binary, "auto"]`, `StartCalendarInterval={"Hour":H,"Minute":M}`, `RunAtLoad=false`, `WorkingDirectory=$HOME`, `EnvironmentVariables=_capture_env()`, `StandardOutPath`/`StandardErrorPath` at `~/.dataclaw/logs/auto.{out,err}.log`. Atomic write, then `launchctl bootout` (ignore "not loaded") + `launchctl bootstrap gui/$(id -u) <plist>`. Fall back to `launchctl unload/load` if `sw_vers` reports ≤ 10.14.
3. **Linux**: write `.service` (`Type=oneshot`, `ExecStart=<binary> auto`, `WorkingDirectory=%h`, one `Environment=KEY=VALUE` line per allowlisted var) and `.timer` (`OnCalendar=*-*-* HH:MM:00`) under `~/.config/systemd/user/`. Then `systemctl --user daemon-reload && systemctl --user enable --now dataclaw-auto.timer`.
4. **`uninstall` + `status`** match the install path.
5. **Subparsers**: `install-schedule --time HH:MM`, `uninstall-schedule`, `schedule-status`. Save `config["auto"]["schedule"]` on success.

### Step 24: Notifications (`dataclaw/scheduler.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **`notify(title, body)`** — `osascript` on macOS, `notify-send` on Linux; swallow failures.

### Step 25: Tests — auto + scheduler (`tests/test_cli.py`, `tests/test_scheduler.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **`test_auto_requires_enable_auto`** — exit 2.
2. **`test_auto_noop_when_no_new_sessions`** — exit 0; `last_auto_run.result="noop"`.
3. **`test_auto_blocks_in_strict_mode_preserves_staging`** — monkey-patch `scan_shards` to return 1 new finding; strict → exit 3; no push call; notification fired; `last_auto_run.staging_dir` set; the run dir still exists on disk.
4. **`test_auto_permissive_appends_redact_rereda cts_and_pushes`** — cutoff advances only after push.
5. **`test_auto_dry_run_skips_push`**.
6. **`test_auto_force_pushes_despite_findings`**.
7. **`test_cleanup_stale_runs_keeps_last_3`**.
8. **`test_auto_threads_device_and_min_score_from_config`** — set `config.privacy_filter={"device":"mps","min_score":0.3}`; spy on `scan_shards`; assert received `device="mps"`, `min_score=0.3`.
9. **`test_install_schedule_macos_plist_contents`** — `Hour`, `Minute`, `WorkingDirectory`, `EnvironmentVariables` (allowlist subset), `ProgramArguments` resolved via `shutil.which`.
10. **`test_install_schedule_linux_systemd_unit_contents`** — `OnCalendar`, `ExecStart`, `WorkingDirectory=%h`, `Environment=` per var.
11. **`test_capture_env_filters_to_allowlist`**.

     src-tauri/{tauri.conf.json, Cargo.toml, src/{main.rs, dataclaw.rs}}

### Step 27: Rust backend — CLI bridge (`app/src-tauri/src/dataclaw.rs`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Binary resolution order**: `$DATACLAW_BIN` → `which dataclaw` → `~/.local/bin/dataclaw` → `/opt/homebrew/bin/dataclaw` → error tile.
2. **Commands** (consume the new CLI surface):
   - `dataclaw_status`, `dataclaw_config_get(show_secrets: bool)`, `dataclaw_config_set_redact(values)`, `dataclaw_config_remove_redact(values)`, same pairs for `redact_usernames` / `excluded_projects`, `dataclaw_config_assign` / `dataclaw_config_unassign`, `dataclaw_auto_now(force)`, `dataclaw_install_schedule(time_hhmm)` / `dataclaw_uninstall_schedule` / `dataclaw_schedule_status`.
3. **`parse_json_block(bytes)`** — extract `---DATACLAW_JSON---`; fall back to full stdout.

### Step 28: Frontend (`app/src/routes/*.tsx`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Dashboard** — last-run card (timestamp, result, sessions, repo URL, `staging_dir` when blocked), **Run now**, **Open findings**.
2. **Config** — form for repo, source, folder-rules buckets, redact lists (add + remove), redact-usernames, excluded projects, auto policy, schedule time. All writes via new CLI verbs.
3. **Findings** — list `pf_new` rows with "Add to redact list" quick-action that calls `dataclaw_config_set_redact` with the union.
4. **Tray** — `TrayIconBuilder` with `[Open, Run now, View last run, -, Quit]`; left-click toggles panel.

### Step 29: Packaging + tests (`app/`, `app/src-tauri/src/dataclaw.rs`)
**Scope:** Small
1. `pnpm tauri build` → `.dmg` / `.AppImage`; privacy-filter model NOT bundled.
2. **Rust unit tests** for `parse_json_block` and binary-resolution order (`$DATACLAW_BIN` points at a shell stub).
3. Frontend is `info` priority; manual QA via `pnpm tauri dev`.

---

## Execution Order

1. **Phase 1** — schema + deps + cooled filter + ISO parsing. Backward-compat; unblocks everything.
2. **Phase 2** — sharded export with per-run staging, dedup, and manifest-scoped upload. Validate same-day-merge behavior with a scratch HF repo before wiring auto-mode.
3. **Phase 3** — buckets + config-CLI verbs + copy sync. Do the copy sync now so Phase 6 isn't blocked.
4. **Phase 4** — privacy-filter behind `pii` extra; run the floor-verification (Step 21) before merging.
5. **Phase 5** — auto + scheduler.
6. **Phase 6** — Tauri last, consumes the stable CLI contract.

## Validation Order

1. **Unit tests per phase** — `pytest tests/`.
2. **Legacy parity** — `test_export_to_jsonl_flat_path_unchanged_byte_for_byte`, `test_push_to_huggingface_legacy_signature_unchanged`, `test_confirm_on_single_file_unchanged`.
3. **Same-day merge smoke** (script): two `dataclaw export --sharded` runs 5 minutes apart with the cooled filter disabled; assert the combined shard file contains all sessions from both runs with no duplicates.
4. **Stale-upload guard smoke** (script): leave a stale `.jsonl` in an earlier run dir; verify `push_shards_to_huggingface` (called on the *current* run dir) does NOT upload the stale path.
5. **Contract test** — `pytest -m pii tests/test_privacy_filter.py::test_pipeline_decoder_matches_model_card_examples` after installing `dataclaw[pii]`.
6. **HF smoke** — push to scratch repo; `load_dataset(repo, "claude")` + `load_dataset(repo)` both succeed.
7. **Auto smoke** — `enable-auto` → `install-schedule --time <2m-from-now>` → wait → inspect `~/.dataclaw/logs/auto.out.log`.
8. **Tauri manual QA** (`info`): `pnpm tauri dev`, exercise Run now, Config save (replace + remove), install schedule.

## Risk & tradeoff notes

- **Same-day merge via HF round-trip**: each run that writes to a shard path that already exists on HF performs one `hf_hub_download`. For a typical user with 5–15 source directories, this adds < 3 seconds per run. On network failure, the run aborts rather than silently drop prior sessions.
- **Per-run staging dir vs single persistent dir**: per-run isolates blast radius and lets blocked runs preserve their context for `dataclaw confirm <run_dir>` without contaminating the next run. Cost: GC keeps the 3 most recent runs.
- **Manifest-scoped upload**: explicit `allow_patterns` list of exact paths means stale files cannot be pushed even if they exist in the run dir. Trade-off: uploading 50+ shards adds ~50 strings to the commit's allow-patterns; well within HF Hub's limits.
- **Pipeline-based decoding (locked)**: swapped for hand-rolled Viterbi only if the canonical-inputs contract test (Step 20.5) fails on a minor Transformers release. Fingerprint-based diffing hashes final text spans, so minor decoder-calibration differences don't change the new/known set in practice.
- **Transformers floor**: preflight verification (Step 21) runs at PR time; floor will be bumped if `>=4.57` doesn't actually load the arch.
- **Staging env leaks**: `_ENV_ALLOWLIST` limits what lands in the plist/systemd unit. `HF_TOKEN`, if set, gets copied — this is the same trust boundary as `~/.dataclaw/config.json` (user's own home, 0600).

Bump the floor to the earliest release that includes the arch if 4.57 doesn't load it. Record verified version in the PR description and CHANGELOG."
    },
    {
      "id": "FLAG-002",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Per the gate's explicit guidance after 3 consecutive iterations (\"dispute-eligible critic churn; pipeline-simple rationale is engineering-sound\"), the architecture stays with `transformers.pipeline(aggregation_strategy='simple')`. Added a canonical-inputs contract test (Step 20.5) that loads the real model and verifies expected entity kinds on model-card example strings \u2014 replaces speculative BIOES/Viterbi debate with a behavioral gate. If the test ever fails on a minor Transformers release, we bump the floor or swap `aggregation_strategy` without changing the public API."
    },
    {
      "id": "correctness-2",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Same as FLAG-002 \u2014 locked the decoder choice with an empirical contract test rather than hand-rolled Viterbi. The plan's Step 17 docstring explicitly states the rationale and points to the contract test."
    },
    {
      "id": "FLAG-006",
      "resolution": "addressed",
      "reason": "Same as FLAG-002/correctness-2 \u2014 empirical contract test against model-card inputs, marked `@pytest.mark.pii` to run only with the optional extra installed. This is the minimum-viable proof-of-behavior without needing to replicate Viterbi decoding byte-for-byte."
    },
    {
      "id": "issue_hints-2",
      "resolution": "rejected",
      "reason": "Meta-flag misreads the workflow: this is a planning step whose defined output is a plan document handed to a separate implementation worker next. Producing code here would violate the step boundary. Gate rationale at iteration 1 explicitly confirmed this is a workflow misread rather than a plan defect."
    }
  ],
  "questions": [
    "Auto-mode default policy: plan defaults to `strict` (new privacy-filter findings block push; user acks or uses `--force`). Alternative is `permissive` (auto-redact findings inline, push, surface after). Recommendation: strict, because publishing is irreversible. Confirm, or override to permissive.",
    "Folder-rules specification style: plan defaults to explicit `folder_rules.buckets = {name: [project, ...]}` with optional tag fallback. Alternative is tag-only (every project must be tagged before any bucket works). Recommendation: explicit-first. Confirm, or override to tag-only.",
    "Privacy-filter decoder: plan uses `transformers.pipeline(aggregation_strategy='simple')` plus a contract test (Step 20.5) that verifies behavior on canonical model-card inputs. Model card describes constrained Viterbi. Contract test is the empirical gate. If the test passes on the target Transformers release, is this sufficient, or do you want a hand-rolled Viterbi decoder anyway for exact model-card parity?",
    "Transformers floor: plan tentatively pins `>=4.57` based on the model card's 2026-04-22 addition date. Phase 4 preflight (Step 21) verifies the working floor before merge. If you already know the canonical release (4.56 / 4.58 / other), say so to avoid PR-time discovery.",
    "Tauri framework: plan uses React + TypeScript + Vite (standard Tauri 2 template). Swap to Svelte or vanilla JS is ~4h rework. Confirm React.",
    "Same-day merge via HF round-trip: plan calls `hf_hub_download` for each shard path that already exists remotely (< 3 seconds per run typical). Acceptable, or prefer a different collision strategy (e.g. tracking `exported_session_ids` per source in config)?",
    "Blocked run retention: plan preserves the blocked run's staging dir indefinitely (until GC'd when 4th run happens). Alternative: auto-delete blocked dirs after N days. Confirm indefinite retention is fine."
  ],

  "assumptions": [
    "Function signatures for `export_to_jsonl` and `push_to_huggingface` are preserved exactly as today. New sharded behavior lives in parallel functions `export_to_shards(projects, run_dir, anonymizer, config, *, cooled_only, since, fetch_existing)` and `push_shards_to_huggingface(run_dir, repo_id, manifest)`.",
    "Sharded staging uses per-run directories at `~/.dataclaw/export/runs/{uuid4}/`, NOT a persistent staging root. `_cleanup_stale_runs` keeps the last 3 (for debugging); older are deleted. Blocked runs' dirs are preserved and their path is recorded in `last_auto_run.staging_dir` so the user can `dataclaw confirm <path>`.",
    "Same-day shard overwrite is prevented by per-session-id dedup on write combined with HF-hub-aware merge. Before writing a shard path that already exists remotely, `export_to_shards` calls `hf_hub_download(repo_id, path_in_repo, local_dir=run_dir)` to populate the local file; opens in append mode; skips any session whose id is already in the file.",
    "If `hf_hub_download` raises a network error (not a 404), `export_to_shards` aborts the run rather than silently dropping prior sessions. 404 / repo-not-found is treated as first-time push and continues without the merge.",
    "`push_shards_to_huggingface` uses `upload_folder(folder_path=run_dir, allow_patterns=[s['path'] for s in manifest['shards']])` \u2014 exact path list, not a glob. Stale files in the run dir cannot be uploaded.",
    "`device` and `min_score` are keyword-only params on every public entry point of `dataclaw/privacy_filter.py`. Call sites in `confirm` and `auto` explicitly read `config['privacy_filter']['device']` and `config['privacy_filter']['min_score']` and pass them through. Prevents config-value drift.",
    "Privacy-filter decoder uses `transformers.pipeline('token-classification', aggregation_strategy='simple')`. A contract test (`test_pipeline_decoder_matches_model_card_examples`, marked `@pytest.mark.pii`) loads the real model and verifies expected entity kinds on canonical inputs \u2014 empirical proof of behavior replacing speculative BIOES-vs-Viterbi discussion. If it ever fails on a minor Transformers release, the floor gets bumped or `aggregation_strategy` is swapped.",
    "`transformers>=4.57` is the working floor. Phase 4 preflight (Step 21) runs `pip install 'transformers>=4.57' && python -c 'from transformers import OpenaiPrivacyFilterForTokenClassification' && python -c 'pipeline(...)'` and bumps the floor to the earliest release shipping the arch if 4.57 doesn't load it. Verified floor is recorded in the PR description.",
    "Base `pip install dataclaw` does not import `torch` or `transformers`; enforced by `tests/test_import_isolation.py` via subprocess.",
    "Config CLI gets `--set-redact` / `--remove-redact` / `--set-redact-usernames` / `--remove-redact-username` / `--set-excluded` / `--remove-excluded` / `--show-secrets` verbs plus bucket/tag set+clear verbs, so the Tauri GUI can round-trip sensitive list values.",
    "Bucket rules match project identity via `_project_candidates(session)` returning `[_project_dir_name, project, project.split(':',1)[1] if ':' in project else None]`. Every parser (claude / codex / gemini / opencode / openclaw / kimi / custom / subagent-merged) stamps `_project_dir_name` before the filter pass.",
    "Cooled-session filter runs post-parse in `_apply_session_filters` so all source branches including Claude subagent-merged sessions are covered uniformly. Subagent merges stamp `_source_file` as the newest contributing JSONL (`max(mtimes)`).",
    "ISO-timestamp comparison uses `_parse_iso` returning aware datetimes; `None` / unparseable end_times are skipped with one debug line per project; cutoff comparison is strictly `>` on aware-datetime values. Cutoff advances only on push success.",
    "Scheduler captures an env allowlist (`PATH`, `HOME`, `HF_HOME`, `HUGGINGFACE_HUB_CACHE`, `HF_TOKEN`, `LANG`, `LC_ALL`, `TMPDIR`) into both the launchd plist's `EnvironmentVariables` and the systemd unit's `Environment=` lines. `WorkingDirectory=$HOME`. Binary resolved via `shutil.which` at install time.",
    "`dataclaw auto` exit codes: 0 success/noop/dry-run, 2 missing deps or not enabled, 3 policy block, 4 HF push failure. Blocked runs preserve their staging dir and fire a notification pointing at `dataclaw confirm <staging_dir>`.",
    "Tauri app is a thin GUI over the CLI: Rust never writes config or plists directly. Frontend = React + TypeScript + Vite (standard Tauri 2 template).",
    "Two open user decisions (auto-mode policy default, folder-rules spec style) are now surfaced explicitly in a 'User decisions (open)' section in the plan body with recommendations, not just in metadata."
  ],
  "delta_from_previous_percent": 52.27,

  "are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct": [
    {
      "id": "DEBT-003",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: inspected `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/pod.py` and `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/ssh.py`. the existing `_build_ssh_client()` helper returns the package's own `runpod_lifecycle.ssh.sshclient` wrapper, not a raw `paramiko.sshclient`. that means step 0's promise that `pod.open_ssh_client() -> paramiko.sshclient` is a 3-line public wrapper around existing logic is not technically accurate, and step 20's `scp.scpclient(ssh.get_transport())` call would not work against the current wrapper type as written.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-ip-adapter-for-text-a-20260423-2233"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-004",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked `runpodconfig.from_env()` in `../runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/config.py`. it parses `runpod_ssh_private_key`, `runpod_ssh_private_key_path`, and related key vars, but it does not consume `runpod_ssh_password`; the current password path comes from pod ssh details inside `_build_ssh_client()`. the fallback clause therefore misstates the existing config surface when it says it reads the same env vars that `runpodconfig.from_env()` already consumes.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "build-ip-adapter-for-text-a-20260423-2233"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "DEBT-020",
      "concern": "are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked the hugging face dependency currently installed with the repository: huggingface_hub 0.36.2 exposes entrynotfounderror, repositorynotfounderror, localentrynotfounderror, and hfhubhttperror. the plan's step 6 says a 404/not-found target shard should continue and the test stubs repositorynotfounderror, but a missing file in an existing dataset is represented by entrynotfounderror in this api family; if implementation only catches repositorynotfounderror, first-time shard paths in an existing repo can abort instead of being treated as empty.",
      "occurrence_count": 1,
      "plan_ids": [
        "plan-a-major-expansion-of-the-20260424-2243"

Read this file first — it contains 5 checks, each with a question and guidance.
For each check, investigate the codebase, then add your findings to the `findings` array for that check.

Each finding needs:
- "detail": what you specifically checked and what you found (at least a full sentence)
- "flagged": true if this describes a difference, risk, or tension — even if you think it's justified. false only if purely informational with no possible downside.
- Every check must end with at least one finding. Never leave a `findings` array empty. If you found no issue, add one detailed `flagged: false` finding explaining what you checked and why it appears clear.

When in doubt, flag it — the gate can accept tradeoffs, but it can't act on findings it never sees.

Good: {"detail": "Checked callers of nthroot_mod in solveset.py line 1205 — passes prime moduli only, consistent with the fix.", "flagged": false}
Good: {"detail": "The fix handles empty tuples but not single-element tuples which need a trailing comma.", "flagged": true}
Bad: {"detail": "No issue found", "flagged": false}  ← too brief, will be rejected
Bad: {"detail": "The hints suggest approach X but the plan uses Y. However Y is consistent with X's intent.", "flagged": false}  ← a different approach than the hints IS a flag. You found a divergence — flag it. The gate decides if it's acceptable.

After filling in checks, add any additional concerns to the `flags` array (e.g., security, performance, dependencies).
Use the standard format (id, concern, category, severity_hint, evidence). This array can be empty.

Workflow: read the file → investigate → read file again → add finding → write file back. Repeat for each check.

This is critique iteration 3. The template file includes prior findings with their status. Verify addressed flags were actually fixed, re-flag if inadequate, and check for new issues introduced by the revision.

        Additional guidelines:
        - Robustness level: standard. Use balanced judgment. Flag significant risks, but do not spend flags on minor polish or executor-obvious boilerplate.
        - Over-engineering: prefer the simplest approach that fully solves the problem.
        - Reuse existing flag IDs when the same concern is still open.
        - `verified_flag_ids`: list flag IDs from prior iterations that the revised plan actually resolves (e.g., if the plan was revised to fix FLAG-001, and you confirm the fix is correct, include "FLAG-001"). Only include flags you've verified — don't guess.
        - Verify that the plan follows the expected structure when validator warnings or the outline suggest drift.
        - Additional flags may use these categories: correctness, security, completeness, performance, maintainability, other.
        - Focus on concrete issues, not structural formatting.

1. Modify push_shards_to_huggingface in dataclaw/cli.py (Phase 2) to implement exponential backoff: 3 attempts with 30s → 2min → 10min delays on retriable errors (urllib3 exceptions, HTTPError 5xx, 429 with Retry-After honored). Non-retriable (401/403 auth, 400 bad request) fail immediately.

2. Shard writer (export_to_shards) writes to ~/.dataclaw/staging/{run_id}/{bucket}/{source}/{shard_name}.jsonl during the run. On successful push, move the staged shards to ~/.dataclaw/staging/published/{run_id}/ (or delete per config). On failed push, LEAVE them in staging — next auto run's cooled-session filter + cutoff will naturally re-include them (they were never published) so no data loss.

3. Auto mode tracks last_auto_run.push_attempts and last_auto_run.backoff_seconds_total in RUN_SUMMARY.json. If all attempts fail, exit code 4 (HF push failure) and log a structured error event with the final exception. 5. Document in README under Privacy & Redaction: 'If something slips through, DataClaw has a one-click rollback. You can revert your HF dataset to any previous commit from the app or via dataclaw rollback.'

Comprehensive, structured, rotating logs that make failure post-mortems cheap.

Format: JSON lines, one record per event. 2. Log destination: ~/.dataclaw/logs/auto-YYYY-MM-DD.jsonl (rotated daily, keep 30 days via a simple prune-on-startup or TimedRotatingFileHandler). Duplicate WARNING+ to stderr for CLI case; full INFO to file only.
